# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCIES MANDATES (COMPLETE)

## 67. ECONOMIC AND FINANCIAL CRIMES COMMISSION (EFCC)
**MDA Code:** 111009001

The Economic and Financial Crimes Commission (EFCC) is a Nigerian law enforcement agency established on December 12, 2002, under the EFCC (Establishment) Act 2002 (now CAP E1 LFN 2004), and commenced operational activities on April 13, 2003, under pioneer Executive Chairman Nuhu Ribadu. The Commission was created in response to pressure from the Financial Action Task Force (FATF), which had named Nigeria as one of 23 countries non-cooperative in international efforts to fight money laundering. The Commission is a body corporate with perpetual succession and has its headquarters in Abuja with zonal offices across Nigeria.

The EFCC is mandated to investigate and prosecute all economic and financial crimes including advance fee fraud (419 fraud), money laundering, counterfeiting, illegal fund transfers, futures market fraud, fraudulent encashment of negotiable instruments, computer credit card fraud, contract scams, and other financial crimes. The Commission also enforces the provisions of various laws including the Money Laundering Act, the Failed Banks Act, the Banks and Other Financial Institutions Act, and relevant sections of the Criminal and Penal Codes relating to economic and financial crimes.

The EFCC's functions include investigating reports of financial crimes and prosecuting offenders; identifying, tracing, freezing, confiscating, and seizing proceeds derived from terrorist financing and related offences; adopting measures to eradicate the commission of economic and financial crimes; collaborating with government bodies within and outside Nigeria on the eradication of economic and financial crimes; examining and investigating reported cases of suspicious financial transactions; establishing and maintaining a system for identifying, collecting, analyzing, and disseminating information on economic and financial crimes. The Commission has secured numerous convictions and recovered substantial assets, though it has faced criticism regarding political influence and selective enforcement. The EFCC Academy provides training for investigative officers.

## 53. DEFENCE HEADQUARTERS
**MDA Code:** 116002001

The Nigerian Defence Headquarters (DHQ) is the principal headquarters of the Nigerian Armed Forces, situated in the Armed Forces Complex in Abuja, which also houses the Nigerian Army, Air Force, and Naval Headquarters. The DHQ is mandated to drive synergy among the tri-services of the Nigerian Armed Forces and coordinates joint operations. The DHQ was established following the 1979 Nigerian Constitution to enable the Chief of Defence Staff (CDS) carry out his duties. It was abolished by the Buhari regime in 1983 but reestablished and changed to Joint Headquarters (JHQ) in 1985 with the creation of the Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff. In September 1990, the nomenclature reverted to Defence Headquarters.

The Armed Forces Act provides the legal framework for the Nigerian Armed Forces, establishing that the Armed Forces shall be charged with the defence of the Federal Republic of Nigeria by land, sea, and air and with such other duties as the National Assembly may prescribe. The President, as Commander-in-Chief, may give directives on the operational use of the Armed Forces, including for maintaining and securing public safety and public order.

The DHQ's responsibilities include deployment, sustenance, and recovery of forces deployed externally or within the country; coordinating tri-service operations; strategic planning and policy implementation; and ensuring that the Nigerian Armed Forces maintains combat readiness on land, sea, and air. The control of the Armed Forces, their joint operations, and training rest with the Chief of Defence Staff, who coordinates the three Services while the Service Chiefs are responsible for day-to-day running of their respective branches. The DHQ nurtures a professional Armed Forces that is people-centric and capable of meeting its constitutional responsibilities in a joint and collaborative environment. The DHQ conducts joint cooperation ventures and strategic seminars to optimize operational capabilities through transformation and innovation.

## 59. DEFENCE RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT BUREAU
**MDA Code:** 116002002

The Defence Research and Development Bureau operates under Defence Headquarters to coordinate and advance military research and development activities for the Nigerian Armed Forces. The Bureau works to enhance the technological capabilities of Nigeria's defence sector through systematic research, innovation, and development of indigenous defence solutions. It serves as a focal point for identifying research priorities, coordinating research activities across the services, and facilitating collaboration with academic institutions and research organizations.

The Bureau's mandate encompasses conducting and coordinating military research across various domains including weapons systems, communications technology, protective equipment, logistics, and medical research relevant to military operations; identifying emerging technologies and assessing their potential military applications; facilitating technology transfer and adaptation of foreign military technologies to Nigerian conditions; supporting the development of indigenous defence manufacturing capabilities; coordinating with DICON and other defence industries on research-driven product development; establishing partnerships with universities, polytechnics, and research institutes for defence-related research; documenting and disseminating research findings across the armed forces; and advising the defence leadership on technological developments affecting military capabilities.

The Bureau contributes to Nigeria's goal of reducing dependence on imported military equipment by fostering local research capacity and supporting the development of a domestic defence industrial base. It works within the broader framework of the Ministry of Defence's mandate to enhance the capability and sophistication of the country's defence industries.

---

## 54. DEFENCE INDUSTRIES CORPORATION OF NIGERIA (DICON)
**MDA Code:** 116010001

The Defence Industries Corporation of Nigeria (DICON) is the state-run defence corporation established by an Act of Parliament on August 1, 1964, to provide defence materiel for members of the Armed Forces and other security agencies. A West German manufacturing firm, Fritz Werner, was assigned the task of providing technical expertise and establishing the Ordnance Factory in Kaduna. The original facilities were designed with production capacities of 5,000 BM 59 Rifles, 18,000 SMG 12 units, 12,000,000 rounds of 7.62mm x 51, and 4,000,000 rounds of 9mm x 19 annually.

During the Nigerian Civil War (1967-1970), DICON tripled its production rates, making significant contributions to the war effort. Post-war, DICON diversified into civilian products including rural water supply equipment, industrial spare parts, and furniture. The corporation's mission is to operate ordnance factories for the manufacture and supply of arms and ammunition while using excess capacity to support local industries development. Its vision is to continuously produce and improve arms and ammunition quality to meet Nigeria's defence, security, and foreign policy needs to international standards.

In November 2023, President Bola Ahmed Tinubu signed the DICON Act 2023, which repeals previous legislation and expands the corporation's mandate. The new Act empowers DICON to operate, maintain, and control subsidiaries and ordnance factories; establish the Defence Industry Technology, Research, and Development Institute (DITRDI) for scientific and technological advancement; and foster growth of indigenous defence companies within the Military Industrial Complex. DICON has formed strategic partnerships with local and international defence firms for production of ammunition, drones, MESH systems, armoured vehicles, ballistic vests, and other defence equipment. Notable achievements include the 2019 unveiling of the Ezugwu MRAP armoured vehicle and production of medical equipment during the COVID-19 pandemic.

## 57. DEFENCE INTELLIGENCE SCHOOL
**MDA Code:** 116011001

The Defence Intelligence College (DIC), formerly known as the Defence Intelligence School (DIS), was established in 2001 as the training arm of the Defence Intelligence Agency (DIA) under Decree 19 of 1986 (National Security Agencies Act). At inception, it was located at a temporary site within the DIA Headquarters in Bonny Camp, Lagos. Due to the need for larger space and a more conducive environment, the school was relocated to its present location in Karu, a suburb of the Federal Capital Territory, Abuja, in October 2005.

The objective of the relocation was to reposition the school with a focus on capacity building in support of the DIA and the Armed Forces of Nigeria through the provision of real-time defence intelligence to enhance national security. The nomenclature was changed from Defence Intelligence School to Defence Intelligence College in March 2013, thereby encapsulating the Agency's vision to make the institution a citadel of intelligence and security training in Nigeria.

The College's mandate is to provide security and intelligence training for all categories of DIA staff, personnel of the Nigerian Armed Forces, and other security agencies, to enable them to perform optimally wherever they may be deployed. The vision is to develop well-trained, patriotic, and highly motivated manpower working with cutting-edge technology under effective leadership in collaboration with friendly forces, providing comprehensive and timely defence intelligence in support of national security strategy. The College offers various courses in intelligence analysis, collection techniques, counterintelligence operations, surveillance methodologies, and other specialized intelligence tradecraft to build institutional intelligence capacity across Nigeria's defence and security architecture.

## 55. DEFENCE INTELLIGENCE AGENCY (DIA)
**MDA Code:** 116012001

The Defence Intelligence Agency (DIA) is Nigeria's primary military intelligence organization responsible for collecting, analyzing, and disseminating intelligence related to defence matters within and beyond the country's borders. The DIA was established in June 1986 through Decree Number 19 (National Security Agencies Act) under President Ibrahim Babangida's military regime, following the dissolution of the National Security Organization (NSO) and restructuring of Nigeria's security services into three separate entities: the State Security Service (SSS) for domestic intelligence, the National Intelligence Agency (NIA) for foreign intelligence, and the DIA for military intelligence.

The DIA operates under the supervision of the Chief of Defence Staff and is headed by the Chief of Defence Intelligence (CDI), typically a major general appointed by the President. The agency's headquarters is located in Asokoro, Abuja. According to the National Security Agencies Act, the DIA's mandate encompasses protection and preservation of all military classified matters concerning Nigeria's security, both within and outside the country; responsibilities affecting defence intelligence of a military nature as determined by the President or Chief of Defence Staff; and providing strategic military intelligence to support national security objectives.

The DIA's core functions include intelligence collection through human intelligence (HUMINT), signals intelligence (SIGINT), imagery intelligence (IMINT), and open-source intelligence (OSINT); analysis and assessment to provide actionable insights for military commanders and policymakers; strategic warning and early detection of emerging security threats including terrorism, insurgency, and transnational crimes; counterintelligence operations to neutralize threats from foreign intelligence agencies and insider threats; and support to military operations including peacekeeping missions, counterinsurgency campaigns, and border security. The DIA collaborates with other intelligence agencies and international partners, and has been particularly active in providing intelligence support for operations against Boko Haram and other security threats.

## 56. DEFENCE INTELLIGENCE AGENCY CIVILIAN PENSIONS BOARD
**MDA Code:** 116012002

The Defence Intelligence Agency Civilian Pensions Board is established to administer pension matters for civilian staff of the Defence Intelligence Agency. The Board operates under the broader framework of the National Security Agencies Act 1986, which established the DIA, and the Pension Reform Act governing federal public service pensions. The Board is responsible for managing retirement benefits, gratuities, and pension entitlements for non-military personnel serving within the DIA structure.

The Board's mandate includes processing pension applications and documentation for retiring civilian staff; calculating and disbursing retirement benefits in accordance with applicable pension laws and regulations; maintaining accurate records of civilian employees' service years and contributions; liaising with the National Pension Commission and relevant pension fund administrators; addressing grievances and appeals related to pension matters; and ensuring compliance with federal pension policies within the agency. The Board works to ensure that civilian staff who have served the intelligence community receive their entitled benefits upon retirement or disengagement from service.

The establishment of a dedicated civilian pensions board recognizes the unique nature of service within intelligence agencies, where civilian staff play crucial support roles in administration, technical services, and other non-operational functions. The Board ensures these personnel are properly compensated for their years of dedicated service to national security while maintaining the confidentiality requirements inherent to intelligence work.

## 58. DEFENCE MISSIONS
**MDA Code:** 116015017

Defence Missions represent Nigeria's military diplomatic presence abroad, operating under the Ministry of Defence to advance Nigeria's defence interests internationally. These missions are established in strategic locations worldwide to facilitate military cooperation, coordinate defence attaché activities, and represent Nigeria's armed forces in bilateral and multilateral defence engagements. Defence missions work closely with Nigerian embassies and high commissions while maintaining their distinct military diplomatic functions.

The mandate of Defence Missions includes representing Nigeria's defence interests in host countries and international organizations; facilitating military-to-military cooperation and defence agreements; coordinating procurement of military equipment and technical assistance; gathering open-source military and strategic intelligence relevant to Nigeria's defence planning; supporting Nigerian military personnel on training attachments, courses, and official duties abroad; participating in international defence forums, conferences, and peacekeeping coordination; and facilitating official visits by Nigerian military officials.

Defence attachés posted to these missions serve as the primary link between the Nigerian Armed Forces and host nation militaries. They engage with defence ministries, military commands, and defence industries in their jurisdictions to advance Nigeria's military modernization objectives and strengthen international defence partnerships. The missions play important roles in Nigeria's participation in regional security arrangements under ECOWAS and the African Union, as well as cooperation with global partners through the United Nations and bilateral defence treaties.

## 60. DEFENCE SPACE ADMINISTRATION (DSA)
**MDA Code:** 116018001

The Defence Space Administration (DSA), formerly known as the Defence Space Agency, was established on October 9, 2014, pursuant to the Nigerian Space Policy and Programmes (NSPP) approved in 2001, which mandated the creation of a Defence Space Command to facilitate and implement defence and security aspects of the National Space Policy. The DSA Act 2016 was enacted by the 8th National Assembly and signed into law on February 3, 2017, formalizing the agency's mandate and operations.

The DSA's mandate is to promote national security and development, support military operations, and improve the Nigerian Armed Forces' cyberspace capabilities. The agency harnesses space products and services to aid the Nigerian military and other security agencies in tackling security challenges, recognizing space as a force multiplier. The DSA's vision is to create space competence capable of producing and utilizing space assets to meet the operational requirements of the Armed Forces and other security agencies.

The DSA operates through several directorates including Policy, Plans and Research; Navigation, Positioning and Timing; Communication Satellites; Launch Services and Space Operations; Earth Observation; and Cyber Security. Key achievements include the production of over 640 topographic maps supporting various security operations, identification of illegal refining sites, enhanced monitoring of military zones, and improved border security. The agency has established a Geospatial Intelligence Fusion and Data Center and works to develop Nigeria's indigenous rocket engineering capacity. The DSA provides secured satellite communication to the Armed Forces and is establishing Earth observation stations in military formations across all geopolitical zones. The agency is headed by the Chief of Defence Space Administration and operates in collaboration with the National Space Research and Development Agency (NASRDA).

---

## 61. DEFENCE SPACE SCHOOL, OROGUN, DELTA STATE
**MDA Code:** 116018002

The Defence Space School in Orogun, Delta State, is a specialized training institution established under the Defence Space Administration to develop human capacity in space science and technology applications for Nigeria's defence sector. The school provides technical education and training in space-related disciplines to military personnel and other authorized participants, building the expertise needed to operate and maintain space assets and systems supporting the Armed Forces.

The school's mandate includes providing foundational and advanced training in space science and technology applications relevant to military operations; developing technical skills in satellite communications, remote sensing, Geographic Information Systems (GIS), and related technologies; training personnel in the operation and maintenance of space-based systems used by the Armed Forces; conducting courses in cyber security and space domain awareness; supporting the Defence Space Administration's capacity building objectives; and preparing Nigerian defence personnel for specialized roles in space operations.

The establishment of the Defence Space School reflects Nigeria's commitment to developing indigenous capacity in the strategic space domain. The school works to ensure that the Nigerian Armed Forces have trained personnel capable of leveraging space technologies for enhanced operational effectiveness. The curriculum covers both theoretical foundations and practical applications, preparing graduates to support the DSA's mission of utilizing space assets for national security and defence operations.

---

## 65. DIRECTORATE OF TECHNICAL COOPERATION IN AFRICA
**MDA Code:** 119007001

The Directorate of Technical Cooperation in Africa operates under the Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs to coordinate Nigeria's technical assistance and cooperation programmes with other African countries. The Directorate implements Nigeria's policy of sharing technical expertise, knowledge, and resources with fellow African nations as part of Nigeria's commitment to African development and continental solidarity. This reflects Nigeria's historical leadership role in African affairs and its commitment to South-South cooperation.

The Directorate's mandate encompasses coordinating technical assistance programmes to African countries in areas where Nigeria has developed capacity; facilitating the exchange of experts, professionals, and technical personnel between Nigeria and other African nations; managing scholarship programmes for African students to study in Nigerian institutions; organizing training programmes and workshops for participants from other African countries; coordinating Nigeria's contributions to regional and continental technical cooperation initiatives; liaising with the African Union and regional economic communities on technical cooperation matters; and documenting and evaluating the impact of Nigeria's technical cooperation programmes.

Through the Directorate, Nigeria provides technical assistance in various fields including healthcare, agriculture, education, infrastructure, public administration, and security sector development. The programmes reflect Nigeria's commitment to using its resources and expertise to support the development of fellow African nations while strengthening bilateral relationships and Nigeria's diplomatic influence on the continent. The Directorate works with Nigerian ministries, agencies, and institutions to identify areas of technical strength that can benefit other African countries.

## 380. FOREIGN MISSION: ABIDJAN

**MDA Code:** 119009001

Nigeria's Foreign Mission in Abidjan, Côte d'Ivoire (Ivory Coast), is a diplomatic mission representing Federal Republic of Nigeria in Côte d'Ivoire and countries under concurrent accreditation. Located in Abidjan, Côte d'Ivoire's economic capital and West Africa's major financial center, the mission operates under Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The mission promotes Nigeria-Côte d'Ivoire bilateral relations, protects Nigerian citizens' interests, facilitates trade and investment, and promotes Nigeria's foreign policy objectives in francophone West Africa. As ECOWAS member states, both countries cooperate on regional integration, security and economic development.

**Core Mandate:** To represent Nigeria's interests in Côte d'Ivoire, promote bilateral relations, protect Nigerian citizens and advance Nigeria's foreign policy objectives.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Represent Federal Republic of Nigeria in Côte d'Ivoire and countries of concurrent accreditation
(b) Promote and strengthen bilateral political, economic and cultural relations with Côte d'Ivoire
(c) Protect rights and interests of Nigerian citizens in Côte d'Ivoire
(d) Provide consular services including passport issuance, visa processing and citizen registration
(e) Promote trade and investment between Nigeria and Côte d'Ivoire
(f) Facilitate business partnerships and economic cooperation
(g) Implement Nigeria's foreign policy objectives in francophone West Africa
(h) Monitor political, economic and security developments in Côte d'Ivoire
(i) Report to Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs on developments affecting Nigeria's interests
(j) Coordinate Nigeria's participation in ECOWAS activities and regional integration
(k) Promote cultural exchanges and people-to-people relations
(l) Provide assistance to distressed Nigerian citizens
(m) Issue emergency travel documents to Nigerians
(n) Facilitate educational and scholarship opportunities for Nigerians
(o) Promote Nigeria's image and correct negative perceptions
(p) Liaise with Ivorian government on bilateral issues
(q) Coordinate with international organizations based in Abidjan
(r) Facilitate high-level visits between Nigeria and Côte d'Ivoire
(s) Promote cooperation on security, counter-terrorism and border management
(t) Advance Nigeria's interests in multilateral forums in West Africa

## 172. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF INFORMATION AND NATIONAL ORIENTATION - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 123001001

The Federal Ministry of Information and National Orientation (renamed from Federal Ministry of Information in October 2024 cabinet reshuffle, previously Federal Ministry of Information and Culture before Arts, Culture and Creative Economy was separated in 2023) is responsible for government communication, public information dissemination, national orientation, media regulation, public enlightenment and national integration. Led by Minister of Information and National Orientation assisted by Permanent Secretary, the Ministry serves as Federal Government's communication hub managing official information flow. The Ministry supervises Nigerian Television Authority (NTA), Federal Radio Corporation of Nigeria (FRCN), Voice of Nigeria (VON), News Agency of Nigeria (NAN), National Broadcasting Commission (NBC), National Orientation Agency (NOA), Nigerian Press Council, Advertising Practitioners Council of Nigeria (APCON), and National Film and Video Censors Board.

**Core Mandate:** To provide timely and accurate information about government policies, programmes and activities to citizens, promote national unity and cohesion, and facilitate democratic governance through transparent communication.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Disseminate information about Federal Government policies, programmes and activities
(b) Coordinate government communication strategy and official government statements
(c) Oversee Nigerian Television Authority for public broadcasting services
(d) Manage Federal Radio Corporation of Nigeria for radio broadcasting
(e) Coordinate Voice of Nigeria for international broadcasting promoting Nigeria's image
(f) Oversee News Agency of Nigeria as national news wire service
(g) Regulate broadcasting industry through National Broadcasting Commission
(h) Coordinate national orientation programmes promoting patriotism through National Orientation Agency
(i) Promote civic education and citizenship responsibilities
(j) Coordinate public enlightenment campaigns on government policies
(k) Regulate advertising practice through APCON
(l) Regulate print media and journalism practice through Nigerian Press Council
(m) Coordinate film censorship and video censorship through National Film and Video Censors Board
(n) Organize press briefings, media engagements and ministerial press conferences
(o) Facilitate media access to government information and officials
(p) Promote press freedom and protect journalists' rights
(q) Coordinate government's digital communication and social media strategy
(r) Promote national integration and inter-ethnic harmony
(s) Counter fake news, misinformation and disinformation
(t) Coordinate national campaigns on critical issues including anti-corruption and peace building

## 232. FEDERAL RADIO CORPORATION OF NIGERIA

**MDA Code:** 123004001

The Federal Radio Corporation of Nigeria (FRCN) is the national public radio broadcasting organization established to provide radio services across Nigeria. Operating multiple radio stations nationwide including national services (Radio Nigeria), zonal stations and state stations, FRCN broadcasts in English, major Nigerian languages (Hausa, Yoruba, Igbo) and minority languages. Established under Decree 8 of 1978 (Federal Radio Corporation of Nigeria Act), FRCN operates under Federal Ministry of Information and National Orientation providing news, current affairs, education, entertainment and cultural programming. FRCN complements Nigerian Television Authority in public broadcasting.

**Core Mandate:** To provide quality radio broadcasting services for information, education, entertainment and national integration across Nigeria.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Operate national radio services broadcasting to entire Nigeria
(b) Operate zonal radio stations serving geo-political zones
(c) Broadcast news, current affairs and information programmes
(d) Provide educational broadcasting including schools programmes
(e) Broadcast entertainment including music, drama and comedy
(f) Promote Nigerian languages and cultures through vernacular broadcasting
(g) Broadcast in major Nigerian languages (Hausa, Yoruba, Igbo) and minority languages
(h) Provide emergency broadcasting during national crises and disasters
(i) Broadcast government policies, programmes and public service announcements
(j) Operate external service (Voice of Nigeria) for international broadcasting
(k) Promote national unity, integration and patriotism through programming
(l) Broadcast agricultural programmes for rural farmers
(m) Provide health education and public health information
(n) Broadcast sports including live coverage of national and international events
(o) Promote Nigerian music, arts and cultural heritage
(p) Provide platform for public discourse and citizen engagement
(q) Maintain radio transmission infrastructure nationwide
(r) Train radio broadcasters, producers and technicians
(s) Ensure balanced and objective news reporting
(t) Generate revenue through commercial advertising while maintaining public service mandate

## 129. FEDERAL FIRE SERVICE

**MDA Code:** 124007001

The Federal Fire Service (FFS) was established by an Act of Parliament in April 1963, tracing origins to 1901 when Fire Service in Nigeria was established as arm of Lagos Police Fire Brigade. Operating under Fire Service Act (CAP F17 LFN 2004), the Federal Fire Service is under supervision of Ministry of Interior through Civil Defence, Fire, Immigration and Prisons Services Board (CDFIPB) established by Decree No.14 of July 1986. The Federal Government reform of Fire Services in 2007 significantly redefined the Service's mandate beyond traditional firefighting to include regulatory and policy functions. The Federal Fire Service consists of Federal Fire Commissioner (responsible for efficient administration and government of the Service under Minister's general orders and directions) and such other officers and firemen of various ranks appointed by Federal Civil Service Commission. Headquarters located at Mohammadu Buhari Way, Area 10, Garki, Abuja, with twelve zonal commands distributed nationwide. The Fire Service's core mandate encompasses firefighting, fire prevention, rescue operations and regulatory oversight of fire services nationally. Under 2007 reform, specific functions include: (a) to extinguish, control and prevent fire for saving and protection of life and property; (b) to perform humanitarian and rescue works as required under Minister's authority; (c) to regulate State and Private Fire Services throughout Nigeria; (d) to prescribe and monitor standards for promotion of fire education, fire prevention, fire safety, mitigation and investigation; (e) to ensure effective discharge of functions by State Fire Services; (f) to provide policy direction for the Nation's Fire Services; (g) to ensure standardization of fire and rescue equipment manufactured or imported into Nigeria; (h) to encourage establishment by State Fire Services of adequate number of fire stations and fire posts; (i) to establish National Fire Academy and other training facilities for training firefighters within Nigeria and African sub-region; (j) to formulate and develop training curricula and requirements for fire fighters; (k) to provide paramedical and information services during emergencies; (l) to conduct topography of allotted areas and jurisdictions; (m) to effect rescues during fire incidents, accidents and disasters; (n) to collect and collate fire statistics for planning, research and policy formulation; (o) to inspect and ensure fire safety compliance in public buildings and facilities; (p) to provide fire safety education and public enlightenment programmes; (q) to ensure preparedness and impact reduction during emergencies through adequate planning; (r) to maintain Fire Service Reward Fund; (s) to charge fees for services other than extinguishing fires at prescribed rates; (t) to coordinate national emergency responses; and (u) to make fire-fighting and fire prevention services available to non-members through training facilities. Fire Service shall not be used for suppressing civil disturbances. The Service maintains vision to produce firefighters proficient in prevention and mitigation of fire and other emergencies.

## 130. FEDERAL GOVT STAFF HOUSING LOANS BOARD

**MDA Code:** 125002001

The Federal Government Staff Housing Loans Board is a federal government agency established to provide housing loan facilities and mortgage financing to federal civil servants and public officers to enable them acquire or construct residential accommodation. Operating under the Federal Ministry of Works and Housing (now Federal Ministry of Housing and Urban Development), the Board was established to address housing needs of federal government employees and facilitate home ownership among public servants. The Board's core mandate is to administer housing loan schemes for federal government staff and provide affordable financing for residential property acquisition and construction. Specific functions include: (a) to provide housing loans to eligible federal government employees for purchase or construction of residential houses; (b) to administer and manage housing loan schemes and mortgage facilities for federal civil servants; (c) to determine eligibility criteria, loan amounts, interest rates and repayment terms for housing loans; (d) to process housing loan applications from federal government staff and conduct necessary verifications; (e) to disburse approved housing loans to beneficiaries according to established procedures; (f) to monitor loan utilization and ensure funds are applied for approved housing purposes; (g) to collect loan repayments through salary deductions and other approved recovery mechanisms; (h) to maintain loan portfolio and keep accurate records of beneficiaries and loan accounts; (i) to recover outstanding loans and pursue defaulting borrowers according to established procedures; (j) to establish partnerships with mortgage banks, financial institutions and housing developers to expand financing options; (k) to promote home ownership culture among federal government employees; (l) to provide advisory services on housing matters to federal civil servants; (m) to facilitate access to affordable housing finance for public officers; (n) to collaborate with relevant housing development agencies to expand housing supply for federal workers; (o) to prepare and manage budgets for housing loan schemes; and (p) to submit periodic reports on loan portfolio, disbursements, recoveries and housing loan programme performance to supervisory ministry and relevant authorities. The Board operates to ensure that federal government employees have access to housing finance at concessionary rates, contributing to public service welfare and staff motivation while promoting the housing policy objectives of the Federal Government.

## 254. FEDERAL TRAINING CENTRE, ENUGU

**MDA Code:** 125005002

The Centre provides comprehensive training programmes for federal civil servants including induction training, mid-career courses, management training and specialized programmes. Operating under Office of Head of Civil Service of Federation, the Centre serves federal agencies in South-East and parts of South-South Nigeria. The Centre contributes to building capable federal workforce through continuous learning and professional development.

**Core Mandate:** To provide professional training and development for federal civil servants ensuring efficient and effective public service delivery.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct induction training for newly employed federal civil servants
(b) Provide orientation on public service regulations and code of conduct
(c) Offer mid-career development programmes for serving officers
(d) Conduct senior management courses for directors and senior officers
(e) Provide training in public sector management and administration
(f) Offer financial management training including budgeting and accounting
(g) Conduct human resource management and staff development training
(h) Provide training in project planning, execution and evaluation
(i) Offer ICT training for digital government services
(j) Conduct training in office management and secretarial duties
(k) Provide training in public relations and stakeholder management
(l) Offer ethics and anti-corruption training programmes
(m) Conduct training in effective communication and documentation
(n) Provide strategic planning and policy formulation training
(o) Offer procurement and supply chain management training
(p) Conduct customized training for specific ministries and agencies
(q) Provide training in organizational behavior and team building
(r) Offer training in performance appraisal and management
(s) Maintain training infrastructure and learning resources
(t) Collaborate with Head of Service on civil service reforms

## 257. FEDERAL TRAINING CENTRE, LAGOS

**MDA Code:** 125005005

Federal Training Centre Lagos, Lagos State, is a federal civil service training institution located in Lagos, Nigeria's commercial capital with substantial federal government presence. The Centre provides training for federal civil servants working in numerous federal agencies headquartered or operating in Lagos. ## 76. FEDERAL CIVIL SERVICE COMMISSION (FCSC)
**MDA Code:** 147001001

The Federal Civil Service Commission of Nigeria (FCSC) is a constitutional body established with authority to make appointments, transfers, and exercise disciplinary control over all Federal Civil Servants. The Commission's origins date to the Central Public Service Board created following the acceptance of the Nigerianisation Commission Report of January 1, 1949. The Federal Public Service Commission was established on April 1, 1954, through Section 174 of the Nigerian Order-in-Council (Constitution) of 1954. Upon independence in 1960, the Public Service Commission was entrenched in Nigeria's Constitution.

The Constitution empowers the FCSC to appoint persons to offices (other than those of Permanent Secretary) in the Federal Civil Service; exercise disciplinary control over persons holding such offices; and promote and transfer such persons within the civil service. No officer can be appointed into the Civil Service without FCSC authorization if they have been convicted of a crime or had previously been dismissed or asked to resign or retire from government service.

The Commission's specific functions include representing at Senior Staff Committee meetings of Ministries; reviewing and approving recommendations on disciplinary cases of senior officers; ratifying promotions of officers to senior positions, including conducting promotion interviews and examinations; hearing appeals on matters of appointment, promotion, and discipline; ensuring compliance with appointment, promotion, and disciplinary guidelines; coordinating with the Office of the Head of the Civil Service of the Federation; and maintaining standards of professionalism and meritocracy in the civil service. The FCSC plays a crucial role in ensuring that Nigeria's federal civil service operates on principles of merit, fairness, and due process, though its jurisdiction has been reduced over time as various agencies and services have been placed under separate boards.

---

## 75. FEDERAL CHARACTER COMMISSION (FCC)
**MDA Code:** 149001001

The Federal Character Commission (FCC) is an agency of the Federal Republic of Nigeria established by Act No. 34 of 1996 (now CAP F7 LFN 2004) to implement and enforce the Federal Character Principle of fairness and equity in the distribution of public posts and socio-economic infrastructures among the various federating units. The Commission is one of fourteen federal executive bodies established by Section 153 of the 1999 Constitution. The Commission comprises a chairman and representatives of each state of the Federation and the Federal Capital Territory, all appointed by the President subject to Senate confirmation.

The Commission's statutory functions under Section 4 of the FCC Act include working out an equitable formula for the distribution of all cadres of posts in the civil and public services of the Federation and States, the armed forces, the Nigeria Police Force and other security agencies, government-owned bodies corporate, and extra-ministerial departments and parastatals; promoting, monitoring, and enforcing compliance with the principles of proportional sharing of all bureaucratic, economic, media, and political posts at all levels of government; taking legal measures including prosecuting heads or staff of ministries or agencies that fail to comply with federal character principles; advising governments on redressing problems of imbalances and reducing fears of relative deprivation and marginalisation; and ensuring clear criteria and conditions for appointments are established.

The FCC monitors recruitment exercises across federal establishments to ensure equitable distribution among states and the FCT. The Commission categorizes representation into adequately represented (2.5-3%), under-represented (1.5-2.4%), grossly under-represented (under 1.5%), and over-represented (above 3%) status. The Commission's mandate extends to ensuring fairness in the distribution of socio-economic amenities and infrastructure across the federation, addressing historical inequalities, and promoting national unity through equitable access to opportunities.

## 178. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF POLICE AFFAIRS

**MDA Code:** 155001001

The Federal Ministry of Police Affairs was established in 1999 when President Olusegun Obasanjo separated police affairs from Interior Ministry. The Ministry is responsible for police policy formulation, police welfare, budget management, logistics and support services for Nigeria Police Force, while operational control remains with Inspector-General of Police under Police Act 2020. Led by Minister of Police Affairs assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary, the Ministry serves as liaison between Federal Government and Nigeria Police Force, providing policy guidance and administrative support without interfering in operational command structure. The Ministry manages police budget, procures equipment, develops police infrastructure including barracks and training facilities, and coordinates police reforms.

**Core Mandate:** To formulate policies for effective policing, provide logistical support to Nigeria Police Force, manage police budget and ensure police welfare.

**Statutory Functions:**

(b) Prepare and manage Nigeria Police Force annual budget
(c) Provide logistics and equipment for police operations

(f) Coordinate police welfare including salaries, allowances, pensions and insurance
(g) Facilitate police training and capacity building programmes
(h) Coordinate police academy and police colleges development
(i) Promote police-community relations and community policing

(k) Coordinate implementation of Police Act and regulations

The Ministry coordinates Nigeria's digital transformation agenda and oversees Nigerian Communications Commission (NCC), National Information Technology Development Agency (NITDA), Nigerian Postal Service (NIPOST), National Broadcasting Commission (NBC), and manages spectrum allocation and universal service provision.

## 64. DIRECTORATE OF STATE SECURITY SERVICE
**MDA Code:** 157002001

The Department of State Services (DSS), formerly known as the State Security Service (SSS) or Directorate of State Security Service, is Nigeria's domestic intelligence agency established in June 1986 through Decree Number 19 (National Security Agencies Act) under President Ibrahim Babangida's military regime. The agency was created following the dissolution of the National Security Organization (NSO), which was restructured into three separate entities: the SSS for domestic intelligence, the National Intelligence Agency (NIA) for foreign intelligence, and the Defence Intelligence Agency (DIA) for military intelligence.

According to the National Security Agencies Act, the State Security Service is charged with responsibility for the prevention and detection of any crime against the internal security of Nigeria; the protection and preservation of all non-military classified matters concerning the internal security of Nigeria; and such other responsibilities affecting internal security within Nigeria as the National Assembly or the President may deem necessary. The principal officer of the agency reports directly to the President of Nigeria.

The DSS operates as Nigeria's primary internal security and counterintelligence agency, conducting intelligence operations to protect the nation from domestic threats including terrorism, espionage, sabotage, and other threats to national security. The agency provides protective intelligence and security for high-ranking government officials, conducts background investigations, monitors threats to public order, and works to prevent activities that could destabilize Nigeria's constitutional government. The DSS also collaborates with other security agencies on matters of overlapping jurisdiction and participates in inter-agency security coordination. The agency has offices across all states of the federation and maintains a significant role in Nigeria's overall national security architecture.

Operating under Federal Ministry of Transport (previously Interior), FRSC maintains nationwide presence with commands in all states, sector commands, unit commands and outposts. ## 160. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF AGRICULTURE AND FOOD SECURITY - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 215001001

The Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Food Security (FMAFS), formerly Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development (FMARD), is a ministry of the Federal Government of Nigeria established in 1966 (as Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Natural Resources), having passed through serial modifications of appellations to suit prevailing contexts of mergers and demergers with related sectors. The Ministry emerged in 1967 alongside creation of 12 states from 4 regions in post-independence Nigeria, with each state establishing own Ministry of Agriculture and Natural Resources. The Ministry was separated from Water Resources in April 2010, and renamed from Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development to Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Food Security to emphasize food security mandate. The Ministry headquarters is located in Abuja with the current minister being Senator Abubakar Kyari (appointed during President Bola Ahmed Tinubu's administration). The Ministry's core mandate is to formulate and implement policies ensuring food security, promoting agricultural development and facilitating agribusiness for increased food production and employment. With Nigeria possessing over 84 million hectares of arable land (only 40% cultivated), population of over 200 million people (Africa's largest market), 230 billion cubic meters of water, and abundant rainfall in over two-thirds of territory, the Ministry works to harness these resources for agricultural transformation. Specific functions include: (a) to ensure food security in crop production, livestock and fisheries for Nigeria's growing population; (b) to formulate and implement national agricultural policies and strategies; (c) to stimulate agricultural employment and create jobs across agricultural value chains; (d) to promote production and supply of raw materials to agro-allied industries; (e) to provide markets for products of industrial sector through agricultural linkages; (f) to generate foreign exchange through agricultural exports; (g) to facilitate rural socio-economic development throughout Nigeria; (h) to manage agricultural sector through multi-stakeholder engagements to develop commodity value chains; (i) to promote agro-industrial development for economic diversification; (j) to empower farmers through extension services, credit facilities and input supply; (k) to facilitate market access for agricultural products domestically and internationally; (l) to promote sustainable agricultural practices and environmental conservation; (m) to supervise and coordinate agricultural research institutes including National Root Crops Research Institute, National Animal Production Research Institute (NAPRI), National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI), Rubber Research Institute of Nigeria (RRIN), and others; (n) to oversee Federal Colleges of Agriculture, Forestry, Fisheries, Horticulture and other agricultural training institutions; (o) to coordinate agricultural projects supported by donor partners including World Bank, African Development Bank, FAO and bilateral partners; (p) to develop strategic partnerships to stimulate investments driving market-led agricultural transformation; (q) to focus on value chains where Nigeria has comparative advantage; (r) to implement agricultural mechanization programmes including tractor production and distribution; (s) to manage strategic grain reserves and food storage facilities; (t) to coordinate fertilizer production, procurement and distribution; (u) to promote cooperative movements and farmer organizations; (v) to establish and maintain agricultural statistics, planning and policy analysis systems; (w) to facilitate land resources development and soil conservation; and (x) to achieve vision of hunger-free Nigeria through agricultural sector that drives income growth, accelerates food and nutritional security, generates employment and transforms Nigeria into leading player in global food markets.

---

## 125. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF PRODUCE INSPECTION AND STORED PRODUCTS TECHNOLOGY, KANO

**MDA Code:** 215002001

The Federal College of Produce Inspection and Stored Products Technology, Kano, is a specialized federal tertiary institution established to provide training in agricultural produce inspection, quality control, grading, standardization and stored products technology. Located in Kano, Nigeria's major agricultural trading hub and commercial center for northern Nigeria, the college operates under Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development through the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN). The college was established to address critical national need for trained inspectors, graders and technologists capable of ensuring quality standards for agricultural produce intended for export, domestic consumption and storage, particularly given Kano's strategic position as groundnut pyramid capital and major agricultural commodity trading center. Accredited by National Board for Technical Education (NBTE), the college awards National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) certificates. The college's core mandate is to train middle-level manpower in produce inspection, quality assurance, grading, standardization and stored products technology for agricultural commodities. Specific functions include: (a) to provide specialized training in produce inspection and stored products technology through National Diploma and Higher National Diploma programmes; (b) to train technicians and technologists in agricultural commodity grading, classification and standardization based on international and national quality standards; (c) to provide education in produce inspection techniques, sampling methods and quality assessment for grains, legumes, oilseeds and export crops; (d) to train personnel in stored products protection, pest management in storage, fumigation techniques and post-harvest loss prevention; (e) to educate students in warehouse management, storage facilities design and stored grain ecosystem management; (f) to provide training in commodity quality assurance, chemical analysis of agricultural products and food safety testing; (g) to train personnel in export produce inspection, certification and compliance with international phytosanitary standards; (h) to educate students in agricultural produce marketing, commodity trading and market information systems; (i) to provide training in post-harvest handling, drying, cleaning, packaging and preservation of agricultural commodities; (j) to train technologists in integrated pest management for stored products, biological control and safe pesticide application; (k) to educate students in food processing technology and value addition for agricultural commodities; (l) to conduct research in stored products protection, post-harvest technology and quality improvement; (m) to provide consultancy and testing services for agricultural commodity quality assessment; and (n) to train personnel supporting agricultural export development and domestic food security through proper produce handling and storage.

## 270. FEDERAL UNIVERSITY OF AGRICULTURE, ABEOKUTA

**MDA Code:** 215012001

Federal University of Agriculture Abeokuta (FUNAAB), Ogun State, is Nigeria's premier specialized agriculture university established January 1, 1988 following demerger of Universities of Technology. Located in Abeokuta on 10,000 hectares, FUNAAB operates with tripodal mandate of teaching, research and extension services. The university has eleven colleges offering 179 academic programmes (44 undergraduate, 135 postgraduate including 22 PGD, 57 Master's, 56 PhD). Operating under Federal Ministry of Education and NUC regulation, FUNAAB is ranked Africa's top agricultural university with several faculty in world's top 2% scientists. The university conducts cutting-edge agricultural research addressing food security, climate change and sustainable agriculture.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized agricultural education, conduct innovative agricultural research and deliver extension services advancing agriculture and food security in Nigeria.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide undergraduate programmes in agricultural sciences across eleven colleges
(b) Offer postgraduate education up to PhD level in agricultural disciplines
(c) Conduct agricultural research on crop improvement, livestock production and food technology
(d) Provide education in agricultural economics, agribusiness and farm management
(e) Offer programmes in animal science, veterinary medicine and livestock production
(f) Conduct research on sustainable agriculture and climate change adaptation
(g) Award degrees in agriculture, veterinary medicine, environmental management and related fields
(h) Maintain international standards in agricultural education through NUC accreditation
(i) Recruit world-class agricultural scientists and researchers
(j) Operate demonstration farms, livestock units and agricultural research stations
(k) Conduct agricultural extension services to farmers and rural communities
(l) Promote agricultural innovation through Institute of Food Security and Agricultural Research
(m) Establish partnerships with FAO, research institutes and international universities
(n) Provide practical agricultural training for students
(o) Develop ICT for precision agriculture and digital farming
(p) Organize international agricultural conferences and workshops
(q) Establish agribusiness incubation and technology transfer programs
(r) Conduct training for African students through scholarship programmes
(s) Maintain Africa's leading agricultural university status
(t) Contribute to Nigeria's agricultural transformation and food security agenda

## 271. FEDERAL UNIVERSITY OF AGRICULTURE, MAKURDI

**MDA Code:** 215012002

Federal University of Agriculture Makurdi, Benue State, is a specialized federal agriculture university established January 1, 1988 simultaneously with FUNAAB following Universities of Technology demerger. Located in Makurdi, Benue State capital known as "Food Basket of the Nation," the university operates tripodal mandate of teaching, research and extension. The university emphasizes crop production, food science and agricultural mechanization relevant to Benue's agricultural economy. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education and NUC regulation, the university serves North-Central Nigeria conducting agricultural research and training.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized agricultural education, conduct agricultural research and deliver extension services promoting food security and agricultural development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide undergraduate programmes in agricultural sciences and related disciplines
(b) Offer postgraduate education including Master's and PhD in agriculture
(c) Conduct research on food crops, root crops and cereal production
(d) Provide education in crop science, soil science and agricultural engineering
(e) Offer programmes in animal science, fisheries and aquaculture
(f) Conduct research on food technology, preservation and value addition
(g) Award degrees in agriculture, veterinary medicine and related fields
(h) Maintain agricultural education standards through quality assurance
(i) Recruit qualified agricultural scientists and educators
(j) Operate demonstration farms and agricultural research facilities
(k) Conduct agricultural extension to farmers in Benue State
(l) Promote improved farming practices and agricultural technologies
(m) Establish partnerships with agricultural research institutes
(n) Provide hands-on agricultural training for students
(o) Develop agricultural mechanization and farm machinery programs
(p) Organize agricultural exhibitions and farmers' field days
(q) Establish agribusiness centers and agricultural entrepreneurship
(r) Conduct research addressing farmer-herder conflicts through improved farming
(s) Maintain NUC accreditation for agricultural programmes
(t) Contribute to food security and agricultural transformation in Middle Belt

Operating under Federal Ministry of Education and NUC regulation, the university addresses unique agricultural challenges of Niger Delta riverine communities while building institutional capacity.

## 80. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF AGRICULTURE, AKURE
**MDA Code:** 215021001

The Federal College of Agriculture, Akure (FECA), formerly known as the School of Agriculture, Akure, was established on January 10, 1957, by the Government of Western Nigeria. It is one of Nigeria's leading agricultural training institutions, mandated to train middle-level manpower in the field of agriculture. The college is located along Ado-Owo Road, opposite Housing Corporation, Akure, Ondo State, and operates under the Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development through the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN).

The college's vision is to be a world-class agricultural training institution making direct and sustainable impacts on gainful youth employment, food security, and improved livelihoods for farmers. Its mission includes integrating available manpower and resources into agricultural sector opportunities; revolutionizing vocational training and short courses; incorporating value chain concepts and ICT into curriculum; and improving community relations through adopted schools and villages programmes.

FECA has offered both National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) programmes since 1993 in key areas of agriculture including Agricultural Technology, Crop Production, Animal Health and Production, Agricultural and Bio-Environmental Engineering, and other related fields. The college is accredited by the National Board for Technical Education (NBTE) and graduates are eligible for NYSC mobilization. The college maintains crop farms (including cash crops such as cocoa, palm trees, coffee, and rubber), livestock facilities, and demonstration plots for practical training. FECA is a certified master trainer institution in Agribusiness and Value Chain Studies with international partnerships including Bern University of Applied Sciences, Switzerland, and the University of Queensland, Australia. The college regularly conducts vocational training courses, skill development workshops, and capacity-building programmes sponsored by the Federal Ministry of Agriculture for unemployed graduates, farmers, and agricultural stakeholders.

---

## 82. Federal College of Agriculture, Moore Plantation, Ibadan
**MDA Code:** 215022001

The Federal College of Agriculture, Ibadan (formerly School of Agriculture) is Nigeria's premier agricultural training institution, established in 1921 by Mr. O.T. Faulkner, the then Director of Agriculture. The college is located at Moore Plantation, Apata, Ibadan, Oyo State, one of the oldest agricultural stations in Nigeria established in 1899 as a model farm for propagating rubber trees and general agricultural improvement. The station became an experimental facility in 1905 for cotton research directed by British High Commissioner Sir Ralph Moor, after whom it was named.

The institution is supervised by the Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development through the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria established by Act 44 of May 26, 1999.

**Core Mandate and Functions:**
The mandate is to produce diplomats at both National Diploma and Higher National Diploma levels with high academic standards and sound practical skills to meet the demands of the agricultural sector. The college aims to contribute towards achieving Nigeria's goals in agriculture through qualitative education and production of well-equipped middle-level manpower. Core functions include providing qualitative agricultural education including vocational and entrepreneurial training; enhancing agricultural production; serving as a hub for middle-level manpower development in agriculture; providing hands-on training to practically equip students for agricultural processes from input to production and value addition towards food security. Academic programmes span Agricultural Extension and Management, Agricultural Environment and Bio-Engineering, Home and Rural Economics, Horticulture, Animal Production Technology, and related fields.

---

## 81. Federal College of Agriculture, Ishiagu
**MDA Code:** 215023001

The Federal College of Agriculture, Ishiagu (FCAI) is a Federal Government-owned agricultural training institution located in Ishiagu, Ivo Local Government Area of Ebonyi State. The institution traces its origins to the School of Agriculture established in 1955 at Umudike by the Eastern Nigeria Government. In 1964, it was amalgamated with the Agricultural Research and Training Station, Umudike. The institution assumed federal status as the Federal Agricultural Research and Training Station in 1972, becoming the training wing of the National Root Crops Research Institute in 1976.

Following accreditation by the National Board for Technical Education (NBTE) in 1989, the institution became the Federal College of Agriculture, Umudike. Administrative autonomy was granted by the Federal Government in 1991, and the college was relocated to Ishiagu in 1993 following the establishment of a Federal University on its Umudike campus.

**Core Mandate and Functions:**
The college has the national mandate to develop and organise high-quality, practically oriented training with dynamic curricula to produce self-reliant technicians and technologists at National Diploma and Higher National Diploma levels. Its functions include promoting agricultural training, production, and research in Nigeria; providing extension education and services; integrating system-wide innovations to achieve tangible results; pursuing sustainable capacity building, consultancy, and innovative outreach; increasing agricultural productivity for food security; reducing poverty and unemployment; and securing livelihoods through sustainable agriculture and human development to meet national and global challenges. The college collaborates with the National Root Crops Research Institute, Umudike, for research endeavours and offers NBTE-accredited programmes preparing graduates eligible for NYSC participation.

---

## 77. FEDERAL COOPERATIVE COLLEGE, IBADAN
**MDA Code:** 215029001

The Federal Cooperative College, Ibadan (FCC Ibadan) was established in 1943 as a Cooperative Training School, making it the pioneer institution for cooperative education in Nigeria. The school gained College status in 1957 and began awarding recognized certificates in Cooperative Studies, drawing students from Nigeria and across West Africa including Ghana, Sierra Leone, and The Gambia. On April 1, 1976, the Federal Government took over the college, integrating it into national policy on cooperative education. The college is currently located along FAN Milk Road, Eleyele, Ibadan, and operates under the Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development through the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN).

The college's primary mandate is to train middle-level manpower for the cooperative subsector of the economy at National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) levels. Beyond its core mandate in Cooperative Economics and Management, the college has evolved into a multidisciplinary institution offering programmes in Accountancy, Business Administration, Banking and Finance, Computer Science, and Home and Rural Economics. The college also runs Agricultural Technology programmes and plans to introduce additional courses including Agribusiness Management and Agricultural Extension.

FCC Ibadan is regulated by the National Board for Technical Education (NBTE), which has accredited its programmes. Graduates of HND programmes are eligible for the National Youth Service Corps (NYSC). The college maintains the Cooperative Innovation and Empowerment Centre to support the Federal Ministry of Agriculture's agenda to revive and reform cooperative societies in Nigeria. The institution offers short courses, workshops, and seminars for officers and board members of cooperative societies, combining formal academic programmes with practical cooperative development support. The college operates with a strike-free academic calendar and emphasizes practical-oriented education preparing students for careers in cooperatives, finance, and business.

---

## 78. FEDERAL COOPERATIVE COLLEGE, KADUNA
**MDA Code:** 215030001

The Federal Cooperative College, Kaduna was established by administrative pronouncement of the Federal Military Government in 1976 and commenced operations in 1979. The college was established to cater for the growing needs of cooperative education in the northern region of Nigeria, providing manpower for the cooperative subsector of the economy. The college initially took off at a temporary site located at the Educational Resource Centre (E.R.C.) on Gaskiya Road, Zaria, before relocating to its permanent site on Abuja Junction Road, Kaduna.

The college was established as an in-service training institution for cooperative stakeholders and staff of government departments responsible for the supervision of cooperative societies and cooperative development. Initially, trainees were those sponsored by government and cooperative societies. The college started with courses leading to the award of Certificate in Cooperative Studies and later introduced the Diploma in Cooperative Studies to meet increasing demand for higher cooperative education.

The college's programmes have expanded significantly. The National Board for Technical Education (NBTE) accredited the National Diploma programmes with effect from the 2004/2005 Academic Session. The Higher National Diploma (HND) Programme in Cooperative Economics and Management was introduced and accredited in 2008 as a two-year (four-semester) programme. Beyond regular academic programmes, the college runs short courses, workshops, consultancy services, and seminars for officers and board members of cooperative societies and other stakeholders. The college now offers various ND and HND programmes as well as certificate courses designed for skills acquisition and vocational training. The institution aims to equip graduates with transformative educational experiences and prepare them for the labor market and entrepreneurship.

---

## 79. FEDERAL COOPERATIVE COLLEGE, OJI RIVER
**MDA Code:** 215031001

The Federal Cooperative College, Oji River, located in Enugu State, was established in 1976 as one of three federal cooperative colleges created by administrative pronouncement of the Federal Military Government to provide manpower for the cooperative subsector of the economy. The college is situated at Industrial Layout, off the Enugu-Onitsha Old Road in Oji River, and operates under the Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development through the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN).

The college was initially established as an in-service training institution for cooperative stakeholders and government department staff responsible for cooperative societies supervision and development. It started with courses leading to certificates and diplomas in cooperative studies. A significant milestone was achieved in the 1988/89 academic session when the college introduced National Diploma programmes in Cooperative Economics and Management. Currently, the college has four major departments with NBTE accreditation at both ND and HND levels: Cooperative Economics and Management, Banking and Finance, Business Administration, and Computer Science.

The college is regulated by the National Board for Technical Education (NBTE) and supervised by the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN). There are ongoing initiatives to expand programmes to include Mass Communication, Agricultural Technology, Home and Resource Economics, Hospitality and Tourism Management, and Science Laboratory Technology. The college also has plans to transform into a Federal Cooperative Polytechnic through National Assembly legislation. FCC Oji River serves as a host for national training workshops for cooperative stakeholders from the South-East and South-South zones, supporting the Federal Government's Renewed Hope Cooperative Reform and Revamp Programme. The curriculum emphasizes self-belief, self-determination, and market readiness, with mandatory practical entrepreneurship training ensuring graduates become potential business owners.

## 123. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF LAND RESOURCES TECHNOLOGY - OWERRI

**MDA Code:** 215032001

The Federal College of Land Resources Technology, Owerri (FECOLART), was approved by Federal Government in the 1981-1985 4th National Development Plan to address threats of environmental degradation arising principally from soil erosion and flooding in Nigeria. Established in 1980 to train junior and intermediate personnel to participate in checking ubiquitous land degradation problems and ensuring sustainable agricultural production while conserving soil, the college is located at KM4 Old Orlu Road, Egbeada, Owerri, Imo State (temporary site formerly occupied by Conservation Centre for Eastern Zone). Academic activities commenced January 20, 1992, following recommendations of ministerial task force to address initial administrative bottlenecks. The college acquired 100 square hectares at Oforola and Obosima for permanent site, gazetted by Imo State Government in 1998. Operating as a pace-setter institution organized along collegiate system, the college introduced cognate disciplines under supervision of Director, Federal Department of Agricultural Land Resources, Abuja. However, transfer to FEPA/Federal Ministry of Water Resources & Natural Resources in May 1998 created conflict of ownership constituting major setback for infrastructural development. The college's core mandate is to train and develop skilled middle-level manpower in Land Resources Conservation and Environmental Management to reposition agriculture. Specific functions include: (a) to train middle-level manpower in land resources technology, soil conservation and environmental management through National Diploma and Higher National Diploma programmes; (b) to develop and organize short duration courses for relevant serving professionals and private organizations to keep them abreast of modern techniques in land resources management and conservation; (c) to carry out soil advisory services for government and private agencies; (d) to provide education in soil management, soil survey and land evaluation; (e) to train personnel in erosion control, watershed management and land reclamation; (f) to educate students in irrigation and drainage engineering technology; (g) to provide training in agricultural engineering technology for land development; (h) to train personnel in environmental impact assessment and environmental monitoring; (i) to conduct applied research in land degradation control and sustainable land management; (j) to provide consultancy services to individuals, communities, local and state governments in catchment area; and (k) to award National Diploma and Higher National Diploma certificates approved by National Board for Technical Education.

---

## 124. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF LAND RESOURCES TECHNOLOGY, KURU - JOS

**MDA Code:** 215033001

The Federal College of Land Resources Technology, Kuru, Jos, is a federal tertiary institution established to provide specialized education and training in land resources management, soil conservation, and environmental management for the North-Central geo-political zone and Plateau State region. Located in Kuru, near Jos, Plateau State, the college operates under Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development supervision through the Department of Agricultural Land Resources. The college was established following Federal Government recognition of environmental degradation threats arising from soil erosion, gully formation, and land degradation particularly affecting highland and plateau ecosystems requiring specialized trained manpower for sustainable agricultural production and soil conservation. The college's core mandate is to train skilled middle-level manpower in Land Resources Technology, soil conservation and environmental management specifically adapted to plateau, highland and Middle Belt ecosystems. Specific functions include: (a) to provide specialized training in land resources technology through National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) programmes accredited by National Board for Technical Education; (b) to train technicians and technologists in soil management, soil survey, land evaluation and classification appropriate for highland zones; (c) to provide education in erosion control, gully reclamation and watershed management for plateau ecosystems; (d) to train personnel in soil conservation practices, terracing, contour farming and soil erosion prevention in highland agriculture; (e) to educate students in irrigation and drainage engineering for plateau agricultural systems; (f) to provide training in land reclamation, mine site rehabilitation and degraded land restoration; (g) to train personnel in agricultural engineering technology for land development and farm mechanization; (h) to educate students in environmental management, environmental impact assessment and monitoring; (i) to provide training in geographic information systems (GIS) and remote sensing for land resources assessment; (j) to develop and organize short-duration courses for professionals and private organizations in modern land resources management techniques; (k) to carry out soil advisory services, soil testing and land capability assessment for government agencies, farmers and private entities; (l) to conduct applied research in land degradation control, sustainable land management and environmental conservation; (m) to provide consultancy services in land resources planning and soil conservation to communities and government; and (n) to train manpower capable of repositioning agriculture through sound land management practices.

---

## 122. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF HORTICULTURE, DADIN-KOWA, GOMBE

**MDA Code:** 215034001

The Federal College of Horticultural Technology, Dadin Kowa, is a government-owned tertiary institution located at Dadin Kowa, Yamaltu Deba Local Government Area of Gombe State, Nigeria. Approved and established with effect from April 19, 2002, by President Chief Olusegun Obasanjo's administration, the college became the first of its kind in Sub-Saharan Africa. The college is covered by Decree 35 of June 1975 on establishment of Agricultural Research Institutes and conduct of training. Operating as a research institution under the Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN) supervised by Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, the college is accredited by National Board for Technical Education (NBTE) to award National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) certificates. The college's core mandate is to train and improve manpower in Horticultural and Landscaping Technology. Specific functions include: (a) to provide specialized training in horticulture through National Diploma and Higher National Diploma programmes approved by NBTE; (b) to train technicians and technologists in general horticulture, floriculture, vegetable production, orchard establishment and management; (c) to provide education in agroforestry practices related to horticultural systems; (d) to train personnel in horticulture management technology; (e) to provide training in agriculture extension management for horticultural enterprises; (f) to educate students in cooperative economic management for horticultural marketing; (g) to train personnel in science laboratory technology supporting horticultural research; (h) to provide training in fishery technology and animal health and production technology; (i) to offer certificate programmes in general horticulture, floriculture, vegetable production, orchard establishment and agroforestry (1-year duration); (j) to conduct training and empowerment of youth and women in mango, guava and fruit production; (k) to provide training in orchard establishment and management; (l) to offer capacity building in modern farming technology and post-harvest handling techniques; (m) to conduct research in horticultural sciences; and (n) to empower students for labour market and self-employment. As of 2017, the institution trained 1,500 youths from 36 states in agriculture and plant production, having graduated over 1,354 students.
