## 167. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF FINANCE - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 220001001

The Federal Ministry of Finance is responsible for managing Nigeria's public finances, revenue generation, fiscal policy formulation, debt management and economic stabilization. The Ministry coordinates government revenue collection, budget execution, financial regulations and macroeconomic stability. Operating under Minister of Finance (appointed by President, often titled Coordinating Minister for the Economy), the Ministry supervises Federal Inland Revenue Service (FIRS), Nigeria Customs Service (NCS), Debt Management Office (DMO), Budget Office of the Federation, Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), Corporate Affairs Commission (CAC), Pension Commission (PenCom), Nigeria Deposit Insurance Corporation (NDIC), and other financial sector regulators. The Ministry manages Nigeria's relationships with international financial institutions including World Bank, IMF and African Development Bank.

**Core Mandate:** To efficiently manage Nigeria's financial resources, promote economic growth and ensure fiscal sustainability.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national fiscal and financial policies
(b) Coordinate revenue generation through tax administration, customs duties and non-oil revenues
(c) Oversee Federal Inland Revenue Service for tax collection and administration
(d) Manage Nigeria Customs Service for customs duties collection and border control
(e) Prepare and execute Federal Government annual budget
(f) Manage government expenditure and treasury operations
(g) Coordinate public debt management through Debt Management Office
(h) Negotiate external borrowing and manage debt portfolio
(i) Oversee capital market development through Securities and Exchange Commission
(j) Regulate financial reporting and corporate governance
(k) Manage government investments and shareholdings
(l) Coordinate fiscal federalism and revenue allocation to states and local governments
(m) Administer Excess Crude Account and sovereign wealth funds
(n) Manage foreign exchange reserves and exchange rate policies
(o) Coordinate pension fund management and administration
(p) Regulate microfinance and financial inclusion programmes
(q) Oversee insurance industry regulation through NAICOM
(r) Manage government accounting and financial reporting standards
(s) Coordinate public procurement policies
(t) Promote investment climate reforms and ease of doing business
(u) Ensure macroeconomic stability and counter-cyclical fiscal policies

---

## 52. DEBT MANAGEMENT OFFICE (DMO)
**MDA Code:** 220002001

The Debt Management Office (DMO) is a Nigerian government agency established on October 4, 2000, tasked with centrally coordinating the management of Nigeria's public debt. The DMO was created following recommendations from the International Monetary Fund's Debt Management Department after a comprehensive assessment of Nigeria's debt situation in 1999. Initially an autonomous unit within the Presidency, the DMO was transferred to the Ministry of Finance in 2001 and upgraded to a full parastatal with the enactment of the Debt Management Office (Establishment, etc.) Act 2003. The Fiscal Responsibility Act 2007 further strengthened its legal framework.

The DMO's vision is to be a public debt management institution of global reference. Its mission is to meet the government's financing needs in a prudent manner that supports economic development while proactively managing risks associated with public debt. The Office operates based on core values embodied in the principle of R-E-C-I-T-E: Respect, Excellence, Commitment, Integrity, Teamwork, and Efficiency.

The DMO's statutory functions under Sections 6 and 7 of the DMO Act 2003 include advising the Federal Government on terms and conditions of loans, restructuring, and refinancing; maintaining a complete and accurate database of all Federal Government borrowings (domestic and external) including contingent liabilities; preparing annual forecasts of debt service and borrowing capacity; efficiently managing Nigeria's domestic and external debt stocks including financial and currency risks; managing relationships with local and international creditors and investors; issuing and managing Federal Government securities; publishing debt data; and annually advising on financing gaps and borrowing requirements. The DMO has achieved significant milestones including reducing Nigeria's external debt stock, increasing external reserves, improving credit ratings, and successfully issuing Eurobonds, Sukuk bonds, and Green Bonds internationally.

---

## 71. EQUIPMENT LEASING REGISTRATION AUTHORITY
**MDA Code:** 220024001

The Equipment Leasing Registration Authority (also known as the Equipment Leasing Association of Nigeria - ELAN) operates within the financial services regulatory framework to oversee and promote equipment leasing activities in Nigeria. Equipment leasing is a financing method that allows businesses to acquire equipment through lease arrangements rather than outright purchase, improving access to productive assets for businesses that may lack capital for direct acquisition.

The Authority's mandate encompasses registering and regulating equipment leasing companies operating in Nigeria; establishing standards and guidelines for equipment leasing transactions; promoting the development of the equipment leasing industry as a financing alternative; protecting the interests of lessees and lessors in lease transactions; collecting and disseminating data on the equipment leasing sector; advising government on policies affecting equipment leasing; coordinating with the Central Bank of Nigeria, Securities and Exchange Commission, and other financial regulators; and promoting best practices in equipment leasing operations.

Equipment leasing plays an important role in Nigeria's economic development by facilitating access to capital equipment for businesses, particularly small and medium enterprises. The Authority works to create an enabling environment for equipment leasing, addressing issues such as legal documentation, tax treatment, registration requirements, and dispute resolution. By promoting equipment leasing, the Authority supports the acquisition of productive equipment in sectors such as manufacturing, agriculture, transportation, construction, and healthcare, contributing to economic growth and employment generation.

---

## 171. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF INDUSTRY, TRADE AND INVESTMENT - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 222001001

The Federal Ministry of Industry, Trade and Investment is responsible for formulating and implementing policies promoting industrial development, trade facilitation, investment attraction, consumer protection and private sector development. Led by Minister of Industry, Trade and Investment assisted by Ministers of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry supervises Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Commission (FCCPC), Standards Organisation of Nigeria (SON), Corporate Affairs Commission (CAC), Nigerian Investment Promotion Commission (NIPC), National Office for Technology Acquisition and Promotion (NOTAP), Bank of Industry (BOI), Small and Medium Enterprises Development Agency of Nigeria (SMEDAN), Nigerian Export Promotion Council (NEPC), National Sugar Development Council, Raw Materials Research and Development Council, and Export Processing Zones Authority. The Ministry implements National Industrial Revolution Plan and coordinates Nigeria's participation in African Continental Free Trade Area (AfCFTA) and ECOWAS trade agreements.

**Core Mandate:** To formulate policies that promote industrial development, facilitate domestic and international trade, attract investments and ensure competitive markets for economic diversification and growth.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national industrial policies including National Industrial Revolution Plan
(b) Promote industrialization and manufacturing sector development
(c) Oversee Standards Organisation of Nigeria for quality standards and metrology
(d) Coordinate investment promotion and facilitation through NIPC
(e) Implement ease of doing business reforms and investment climate improvements
(f) Regulate business registration through Corporate Affairs Commission
(g) Promote small and medium enterprises development through SMEDAN
(h) Facilitate access to industrial finance through Bank of Industry
(i) Coordinate export promotion and trade development through NEPC
(j) Promote non-oil exports and export diversification
(k) Facilitate trade policy development and trade negotiations
(l) Coordinate Nigeria's participation in regional trade agreements (ECOWAS, AfCFTA)
(m) Promote foreign direct investment attraction and retention
(n) Coordinate technology transfer and acquisition through NOTAP
(o) Promote industrial clusters and special economic zones development
(p) Oversee competition policy and consumer protection through FCCPC
(q) Coordinate value chain development for priority commodities
(r) Promote backward integration policies in key sectors
(s) Coordinate industrial parks and free trade zones development
(t) Facilitate supply chain development and local content policies

---

## 226. FEDERAL PRODUCE INSPECTION

**MDA Code:** 222011001

Federal Produce Inspection is a specialized agency under Federal Ministry of Industry, Trade and Investment (formerly under Federal Ministry of Agriculture) responsible for inspection, grading, standardization and quality control of agricultural produce destined for export and domestic markets. The agency ensures Nigerian agricultural exports meet international quality standards and phytosanitary requirements. Operating produce inspection stations at ports, borders and major agricultural markets, the agency issues quality certificates for exports and enforces produce standards protecting Nigeria's reputation in international trade.

**Core Mandate:** To inspect, grade, standardize and certify quality of agricultural produce for export and domestic markets, ensuring compliance with national and international standards.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Inspect agricultural produce including cocoa, groundnut, palm produce, cotton, rubber and other export commodities
(b) Grade agricultural commodities according to national and international standards
(c) Issue quality certificates for agricultural exports meeting required standards
(d) Operate produce inspection stations at seaports, airports and land borders
(e) Enforce minimum quality standards for exported agricultural produce
(f) Conduct sampling and laboratory analysis of agricultural commodities
(g) Verify compliance with phytosanitary requirements for plant exports
(h) Inspect storage facilities and warehouses for produce quality maintenance
(i) Monitor produce handling, packaging and transportation standards
(j) Prevent export of substandard or adulterated agricultural products
(k) Collaborate with Standards Organisation of Nigeria on produce standards
(l) Coordinate with Nigerian Customs Service on export clearances
(m) Maintain produce inspection laboratories for quality testing
(n) Train produce inspectors and quality control personnel
(o) Develop and update produce grading standards
(p) Conduct market surveillance for produce quality in domestic markets
(q) Investigate cases of produce adulteration and contamination
(r) Promote good agricultural practices and post-harvest handling
(s) Issue export permits for agricultural commodities meeting standards
(t) Protect Nigeria's reputation in international agricultural commodity markets

## 72. EXTERNAL TRADE SECTOR, GENEVA (WTO)
**MDA Code:** 222012001

The External Trade Sector in Geneva represents Nigeria's permanent mission to the World Trade Organization (WTO) and related international trade organizations headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland. This mission operates under the Federal Ministry of Industry, Trade and Investment to advance Nigeria's trade interests and participate in the multilateral trading system governed by WTO agreements.

The mission's mandate includes representing Nigeria in WTO negotiations and deliberations; monitoring developments in international trade rules and their implications for Nigeria; participating in dispute settlement proceedings affecting Nigeria's trade interests; coordinating with other developing countries on common trade positions; reporting on WTO activities and advising the Nigerian government on trade policy implications; facilitating Nigeria's compliance with WTO obligations; engaging with other international trade organizations in Geneva including UNCTAD; and supporting Nigerian businesses in understanding and leveraging WTO agreements.

Nigeria's participation in the WTO is critical for its trade relations given the organization's role in setting rules for international commerce, resolving trade disputes, and negotiating trade liberalization. The Geneva mission engages in negotiations across various WTO committees covering goods, services, intellectual property, agriculture, and development issues. The mission works to ensure that Nigeria's interests as a developing country are represented in trade rule-making, particularly on issues such as special and differential treatment, trade facilitation, agricultural subsidies, and industrial policy space. The mission also coordinates Nigeria's engagement with the WTO's Trade Policy Review Mechanism and technical assistance programmes.

## 128. FEDERAL COMPETITION AND CONSUMER PROTECTION TRIBUNAL

**MDA Code:** 222032001

The Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Tribunal (FCCPT or CCPT) was established by the Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Act, 2018 (FCCPA), which commenced January 30, 2019, repealing the Consumer Protection Act CAP C25, Laws of the Federation of Nigeria 2004. The FCCPA established two institutions for enforcement: Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Commission (FCCPC) and the Competition and Consumer Protection Tribunal (CCPT). The Tribunal is composed of a Chairman (must be lawyer with 10 years post-qualification experience in competition law, consumer protection law, commercial and industrial law) and six other members with 10 years professional experience in competition and consumer protection law, commerce and industry, public affairs, economics, finance, or business administration, appointed by President subject to Senate confirmation. Members serve five-year tenure from confirmation date or until attaining seventy years, whichever comes first. The Tribunal's core mandate is to adjudicate and exercise jurisdiction, powers and authority conferred by the FCCPA throughout Nigeria for promotion of competition and consumer protection. Specific functions and powers include: (a) to hear and determine appeals or review decisions of the Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Commission throughout the Federation; (b) to adjudicate on matters arising from implementation of the Federal Competition and Consumer Protection Act 2018; (c) to hear appeals or review decisions of sector-specific authorities after such appeals have first been heard and determined by the Commission; (d) to exercise jurisdiction over all matters relating to competition and consumer protection as conferred by FCCPA; (e) to impose penalties including administrative penalties, fines and sanctions for violations of competition and consumer protection provisions; (f) to order sale of assets where necessary to remedy anti-competitive conduct; (g) to grant injunctions, make cease and desist orders, and issue other equitable remedies; (h) to review and approve or reject Commission decisions on mergers, acquisitions, monopoly investigations and restrictive agreements; (i) to hear complaints directly filed before it by affected parties; (j) to consider matters referred to it by the Commission; (k) to enforce consumer protection rights and remedies; (l) to adjudicate disputes involving anti-competitive agreements, abuse of dominant position, monopoly situations, misleading and deceptive practices, unfair business practices, merger approvals and price regulations; (m) to make procedural rules governing its operations; (n) to maintain public records and make decisions publicly accessible; and (o) to register its decisions at Federal High Court for enforcement purposes. Appeals from Tribunal decisions go to Court of Appeal within 30 days. Under Section 104 FCCPA, the Act's provisions override all other laws (subject to Constitution) in matters relating to competition and consumer protection, establishing Tribunal's precedence in such matters.

## 174. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 227001001

The Federal Ministry of Labour and Employment is responsible for labour administration, employment services, industrial relations, occupational safety and health, social security and productivity enhancement. Led by Minister of Labour and Employment assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry supervises National Directorate of Employment (NDE), Nigeria Social Insurance Trust Fund (NSITF), Michael Imoudu National Institute for Labour Studies (MINILS), Industrial Training Fund (ITF), Employees Compensation Commission, National Productivity Centre, and registers and regulates trade unions and employers' associations. The Ministry enforces Labour Act, implements minimum wage legislation, mediates industrial disputes and ensures Nigeria's compliance with International Labour Organization (ILO) conventions. The Ministry coordinates tripartite consultations among government, employers and workers.

**Core Mandate:** To facilitate full employment, ensure decent work conditions, promote harmonious industrial relations, enforce labour standards and enhance national productivity.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national labour and employment policies
(b) Regulate employment relationships and enforce labour laws including Labour Act
(c) Promote employment generation through National Directorate of Employment
(d) Manage employment services connecting job seekers with employers
(e) Register and regulate trade unions and workers' organizations
(f) Register and regulate employers' associations
(g) Promote collective bargaining and social dialogue
(h) Mediate and arbitrate industrial disputes preventing and resolving strikes
(i) Enforce minimum wage implementation across sectors
(j) Regulate wages, hours of work and conditions of employment
(k) Enforce occupational safety and health standards
(l) Inspect workplaces ensuring compliance with labour standards
(m) Combat child labour and forced labour
(n) Promote workers' rights and protect vulnerable workers
(o) Administer social security schemes through Nigeria Social Insurance Trust Fund
(p) Manage employees' compensation for workplace injuries through Employees Compensation Commission
(q) Promote productivity enhancement through National Productivity Centre
(r) Coordinate industrial training and skills development through Industrial Training Fund
(s) Regulate employment of expatriates and work permits
(t) Coordinate tripartite consultations among government, employers and workers

**MDA Code:** 228001001

The Federal Ministry of Science, Technology and Innovation (renamed from Federal Ministry of Science and Technology to emphasize Innovation under President Buhari's second term, continued by President Tinubu) is responsible for formulating and implementing policies promoting scientific research, technological development, innovation and technology transfer for national development. Led by Minister of Science, Technology and Innovation assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry supervises over 35 research institutes and agencies including National Space Research and Development Agency (NASRDA), Energy Commission of Nigeria, National Office for Technology Acquisition and Promotion (NOTAP), Raw Materials Research and Development Council, National Agency for Science and Engineering Infrastructure (NASENI), National Biotechnology Development Agency (NABDA), Nigerian Building and Road Research Institute (NBRRI), Federal Institute of Industrial Research Oshodi (FIIRO), and numerous sector-specific research institutes.

**Core Mandate:** To harness science, technology and innovation for economic development, industrialization and improving quality of life.

(c) Promote research and development in biotechnology, nanotechnology, ICT, renewable energy

(e) Coordinate Energy Commission of Nigeria for nuclear science and renewable energy research
(f) Regulate technology acquisition and transfer through NOTAP
(g) Promote raw materials research and development for local utilization
(h) Coordinate engineering infrastructure development through NASENI
(i) Promote biotechnology development and biosafety regulation
(j) Coordinate science and technology education and STEM promotion
(k) Manage science and technology parks, innovation hubs and incubation centers
(l) Promote commercialization of research outputs and intellectual property

(o) Promote indigenous technology development and local content
(p) Coordinate metrology, standards and quality infrastructure
(q) Promote agricultural research, industrial research and health research
(r) Coordinate climate change research and environmental technologies
(s) Manage national research fund and research grants administration
(t) Develop national innovation system and innovation ecosystems

## 131. FEDERAL INSTITUTE OF INDUSTRIAL RESEARCH - OSHODI

**MDA Code:** 228046001

The Federal Institute of Industrial Research, Oshodi (FIIRO), Lagos, is Nigeria's premier industrial research and development institute established to promote industrial and economic development through applied research, technology development and transfer. Operating under Federal Ministry of Science, Technology and Innovation (formerly Federal Ministry of Science and Technology), the institute was established by Research Institutes (Establishment) Order 1977 and operates under Agricultural Research Council Decree 35 of 1975 as amended. Located in Oshodi, Lagos, Nigeria's industrial and commercial hub, FIIRO conducts research across multiple industrial sectors including food processing, chemical technology, biotechnology, pharmaceutical, and engineering. The institute's core mandate is to conduct applied research, provide technical services, develop appropriate technologies, and facilitate technology transfer for industrial development. Specific functions include: (a) to conduct applied industrial research for development of technologies appropriate to Nigeria's needs and resources; (b) to provide technical and consultancy services to industries, government and private sector; (c) to develop and adapt technologies for small, medium and large-scale industries; (d) to conduct research in food processing, preservation, packaging and storage technologies; (e) to carry out research in chemical technology, pharmaceutical products, cosmetics and detergent production; (f) to conduct biotechnology research including fermentation technology, enzyme technology and industrial microbiology; (g) to provide research services in leather technology, textile processing and fiber technology; (h) to conduct research in palm oil processing, cassava processing and other agricultural commodity processing; (i) to develop appropriate technologies for small and medium enterprises (SMEs) and cottage industries; (j) to provide analytical services including food analysis, chemical analysis, microbiological testing and quality assurance services; (k) to conduct pilot plant trials and demonstration of developed technologies; (l) to transfer technologies to industries through licensing, training and technical assistance; (m) to provide training programmes for industrial personnel, researchers, entrepreneurs and students; (n) to establish pilot production facilities and demonstration units for proven technologies; (o) to collaborate with universities, research institutes, industries and international organizations on research projects; (p) to commercialize research outputs and intellectual property developed by the institute; (q) to conduct feasibility studies for industrial projects and technology investments; (r) to provide equipment fabrication, maintenance and calibration services; (s) to publish research findings in journals, technical reports and conference proceedings; (t) to participate in technology exhibitions, trade fairs and industrial development programs; and (u) to advise government on industrial technology policies, standards and development strategies. FIIRO maintains modern research laboratories, pilot plants, analytical facilities and demonstration units supporting industrial research and technology development activities.

## 68. ENERGY COMMISSION OF NIGERIA (ECN)
**MDA Code:** 228073001

The Energy Commission of Nigeria (ECN) was established by Act No. 62 of 1979, as amended by Act No. 32 of 1988 and Act No. 19 of 1989, with the statutory mandate for strategic planning and coordination of national policies in the field of energy in all its ramifications. By this mandate, the ECN is the apex government organ empowered to carry out overall energy sector planning and policy implementation and to promote diversification of energy resources.

The ECN's vision is to ensure adequate, reliable, cost-effective, and sustainable energy supply for the nation's economic and sociopolitical development. Its mission is to promote sustainable energy development in Nigeria through the production of strategic plans and coordination of national policies. The Commission serves as a centre for gathering and dissemination of information relating to national policy in the field of energy.

The Commission's functions include serving as the focal point for energy planning and coordination in Nigeria; promoting the development and optimal utilization of all energy resources including the introduction of new and alternative energy sources such as solar, wind, biomass, and nuclear energy; advising the Federal Government on matters relating to energy policy; coordinating energy-related research and development activities; promoting efficient energy use and conservation; developing standards and codes of practice for energy equipment; collaborating with states and local governments on energy development; establishing linkages with international energy organizations and facilitating technology transfer; and conducting energy data collection, analysis, and publication. The ECN operates through various research centres and works to achieve Nigeria's energy transition goals including tripling renewable energy and doubling energy efficiency targets.

## 184. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF TRANSPORT - HQTRS

The Federal Ministry of Transport is responsible for formulating and implementing policies for all transport modes including road transport, rail transport, maritime transport, aviation, and inland waterways. Following President Tinubu's 2023 cabinet restructuring, aviation and maritime functions were separated into dedicated ministries (Aviation and Aerospace Development; Marine and Blue Economy), with Transport Ministry now focusing primarily on road transport, rail transport and transport policy coordination. (s) Facilitate regional transport integration within ECOWAS framework
(t) Coordinate emergency transport response during disasters

## 162. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF AVIATION AND AEROSPACE DEVELOPMENT - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 230001001

The Federal Ministry of Aviation and Aerospace Development regulates air travel and aviation services in Nigeria. The Ministry was renamed from Federal Ministry of Aviation to include "Aerospace Development" reflecting expanded mandate. Current Minister is Festus Keyamo, CON, SAN, FCIArb (UK), appointed August 16, 2023. Permanent Secretary is Dr. Ibrahim Abubakar Kana, mni. Headquarters located at Federal Secretariat Complex Phase 1, Abuja. The Ministry operates under Five-Point Agenda: (1) ensuring strict compliance with safety regulations and continuous upward movement of Nigeria's ICAO rating; (2) supporting growth of local airline businesses; (3) infrastructure improvement; (4) human capacity development; (5) revenue optimization. The Ministry oversees Nigerian Civil Aviation Authority (NCAA), Federal Airports Authority of Nigeria (FAAN), Nigerian Airspace Management Agency (NAMA), Nigerian College of Aviation Technology (NCAT) Zaria, Accident Investigation Bureau (AIB), Nigerian Meteorological Agency (NiMet), and African Aviation and Aerospace University (AAAU).

**Core Mandate:** To build safe, secured and efficient aviation industry making Nigeria hub meeting international standards and best practices for African continent.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and manage government policies and programmes on aviation and aerospace development
(b) Oversee air transportation including licensing, regulations and operational standards for airlines
(c) Oversee airport development and management through FAAN
(d) Provide aviation infrastructural services including runways, terminals, navigation aids
(e) Provide aviation security services meeting international standards
(f) Improve airspace management and air traffic control through automation and next-generation systems
(g) Ensure safety and security measures comply with ICAO standards and WMO requirements
(h) Regulate civil aviation operations ensuring adherence to safety protocols
(i) Develop and maintain meteorological services for aviation through NiMet
(j) Investigate aircraft accidents and incidents through AIB
(k) Develop human capacity through Nigerian College of Aviation Technology
(l) Certify aerodrome emergency training schools and firefighting facilities
(m) Support growth and sustainability of local airline businesses
(n) Optimize revenue generation from aviation sector
(o) Modernize airport terminals and expand cargo and logistics networks
(p) Position Nigeria as leading aviation hub in Africa
(q) Develop aerospace education and research through African Aviation and Aerospace University

## 179. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF POWER -HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 231001001

The Federal Ministry of Power is responsible for formulating and implementing policies for power sector development, electricity generation, transmission, distribution, rural electrification and renewable energy. Following Electric Power Sector Reform Act 2005 which liberalized sector and created successor companies to defunct National Electric Power Authority (NEPA/PHCN), the Ministry emerged focusing exclusively on power sector. Led by Minister of Power assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry supervises Nigerian Electricity Regulatory Commission (NERC), Rural Electrification Agency (REA), Transmission Company of Nigeria (TCN), and coordinates with privatized generation companies (GenCos) and distribution companies (DisCos). The Ministry implements national grid expansion, promotes renewable energy including solar and wind, coordinates gas-to-power initiatives, and facilitates Nigeria's participation in West African Power Pool.

**Core Mandate:** To ensure availability of reliable, adequate and affordable electricity for all Nigerians, promote electricity access and facilitate power sector development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national power policies and electricity sector policies
(b) Coordinate electricity generation capacity expansion through thermal, hydro, solar and renewable
(c) Oversee transmission infrastructure development and grid expansion through TCN

(e) Coordinate rural electrification programmes through Rural Electrification Agency
(f) Promote renewable energy development including solar, wind, biomass and mini-grids
(g) Facilitate electricity distribution improvements and reduce technical and commercial losses
(h) Coordinate electricity tariff reviews and cost-reflective tariffs
(i) Promote electricity access for off-grid and underserved communities
(j) Facilitate independent power projects (IPPs) and embedded generation
(k) Coordinate power sector reforms and privatization outcomes
(l) Manage federal government-owned generation assets
(m) Promote gas-to-power initiatives leveraging Nigeria's gas resources
(n) Coordinate regional electricity trade through West African Power Pool
(o) Facilitate electricity infrastructure financing and investments
(p) Promote electricity metering and reduce estimated billing
(q) Coordinate demand-side management and energy efficiency programmes
(r) Promote electricity safety standards and regulations
(s) Coordinate electricity market development and Nigerian Electricity Supply Industry
(t) Manage national grid stability and reduce system collapses

The Ministry manages over 35,000 kilometers of federal roads including major highways, interstate roads, and roads connecting state capitals. ## 233. FEDERAL ROAD MAINTENANCE AGENCY

The Federal Road Maintenance Agency (FERMA) was established by Act No. 7 of 2002 (Federal Roads Maintenance Agency (Establishment, etc.) Act 2002) to maintain federal roads network in Nigeria. FERMA operates under Federal Ministry of Works focusing on routine and periodic maintenance of over 35,000 kilometers of federal roads. The Agency conducts emergency repairs, potholes patching, clearing drainage systems, repairing road shoulders and maintaining road furniture. FERMA works to ensure federal roads remain passable between major rehabilitation projects, addressing deterioration caused by traffic, weather and inadequate maintenance funding.

(i) Respond to emergency road failures and accidents affecting road use

The Federal Ministry of Budget and Economic Planning (formerly Federal Ministry of Budget and National Planning) is responsible for national budget preparation, economic planning, development policy formulation and coordination of national development plans. Restructured and renamed under President Bola Tinubu's administration to emphasize integrated approach to budget formulation and economic planning. The Ministry serves as central coordinating agency for Nigeria's development planning and budget management. It oversees National Bureau of Statistics, National Planning Commission, and coordinates implementation of national development frameworks including Economic Recovery and Growth Plan successors.

## 379. FISCAL RESPONSIBILITY COMMISSION

**MDA Code:** 250001001

The Fiscal Responsibility Commission (FRC) was established by Fiscal Responsibility Act 2007 (Act No. 31) to ensure implementation of its provisions promoting prudent management of Nigeria's resources. The Commission operates under the Presidency with mandate to promote transparent and accountable government financial management framework. FRC enforces fiscal discipline, monitors public expenditure, ensures macro-economic stability and promotes economic objectives in Section 16 of Constitution. The Commission monitors Medium-Term Expenditure Framework (MTEF), Excess Crude Account, debt management and budget implementation ensuring fiscal prudence across federal, state and local governments.

**Core Mandate:** To monitor and enforce fiscal responsibility provisions ensuring prudent management of public resources, transparency in fiscal operations and macro-economic stability.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Monitor and enforce provisions of Fiscal Responsibility Act 2007 promoting Constitutional economic objectives
(b) Disseminate standard fiscal practices including international good practices for efficient public expenditure management
(c) Undertake fiscal and financial studies, analysis and diagnosis disseminating results publicly
(d) Monitor operation of Medium-Term Expenditure Framework (MTEF) ensuring compliance
(e) Monitor Excess Crude Account operations and Nigerian Sovereign Investment Authority funds
(f) Ensure revenue-raising policies, resource allocation and debt management decisions are undertaken prudently
(g) Promote transparency and accountability in fiscal operations at all government levels
(h) Monitor federal government compliance with fiscal targets and budget discipline
(i) Compel any person or government institution to disclose information on public revenues and expenditure
(j) Investigate violations of Fiscal Responsibility Act provisions
(k) Forward investigation reports to Attorney-General for prosecution of offenders
(l) Monitor public debt sustainability and borrowing limits compliance
(m) Promote long-term macro-economic stability of national economy
(n) Monitor revenue collection efficiency and expenditure patterns
(o) Ensure budget implementation follows appropriation and cash plan
(p) Promote fiscal federalism and inter-governmental fiscal coordination
(q) Monitor states and local governments fiscal responsibility adoption
(r) Submit annual reports to National Assembly on fiscal compliance
(s) Make rules for carrying out Commission functions
(t) Operate independently in performing functions without interference

The Federal Ministry of Water Resources and Sanitation (renamed from Federal Ministry of Water Resources in October 2024 to emphasize sanitation mandate) is responsible for water resources development, water supply, irrigation, flood control, dam construction and sanitation. Following cabinet reshuffle, sanitation functions previously under environment or health were consolidated with water resources recognizing linkage between water supply and sanitation (WASH sector).

## 169. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF HOUSING AND URBAN DEVELOPMENT - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 253001001

The Federal Ministry of Housing and Urban Development (separated from former Ministry of Works and Housing) is responsible for formulating and implementing housing policies, urban development strategies, physical planning and housing finance. Established to address Nigeria's massive housing deficit estimated at 28 million units, the Ministry promotes affordable housing access, regulates urban development and coordinates urban renewal programmes. Led by Minister of Housing and Urban Development assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry supervises Federal Housing Authority (FHA), Federal Mortgage Bank of Nigeria (FMBN), Urban Development Bank, and coordinates with state governments on housing programmes. The Ministry implements National Housing Fund, National Housing Policy, and Presidential Housing Initiatives.

**Core Mandate:** To facilitate access to decent and affordable housing for all Nigerians, promote sustainable urban development and regulate physical planning.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national housing policies including National Housing Policy
(b) Develop and implement national housing programmes for affordable housing delivery
(c) Oversee Federal Housing Authority for mass housing development and estate management
(d) Coordinate Federal Mortgage Bank operations for housing finance and mortgage lending
(e) Promote National Housing Fund for workers' housing finance
(f) Develop housing finance mechanisms including mortgage insurance schemes
(g) Formulate urban development policies and sustainable urbanization strategies
(h) Coordinate urban renewal and slum upgrading programmes
(i) Regulate physical planning and urban development standards
(j) Promote building standards and construction codes compliance
(k) Coordinate land use planning and zoning regulations at federal level
(l) Develop urban infrastructure supporting housing estates
(m) Promote public-private partnerships in housing development
(n) Coordinate social housing programmes for low-income earners
(o) Develop housing cooperatives and self-help housing schemes
(p) Promote green building practices and sustainable housing technologies
(q) Coordinate housing research and development including building materials research
(r) Regulate real estate development and construction industry practices
(s) Manage federal government housing estates and quarters
(t) Coordinate housing data collection and housing needs assessment

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 241-260

## 241. FEDERAL SCHOOL OF SURVEY, OYO

**MDA Code:** 253017001

The Federal School of Survey, Oyo, Oyo State, is Nigeria's pioneer and only specialized institution for surveying and geoinformatics education. Established in 1908 as Government Departmental Training Institution attached to Colonial Survey Department in Lagos, the institution is older than Nigeria itself (named in 1914). The School moved from Lagos to Okene, then Ibadan (1926), finally settling in Oyo (1934). Became federal institution under Federal Ministry of Works and Housing in 1967. Located on 250 hectares in Oyo, the School awards National Diploma and Higher National Diploma in Surveying and Geoinformatics. As first modern surveying training institution in Nigeria and Africa, approximately 80-85% of Nigeria's registered professional surveyors are products of this institution. The School has trained over 27,500 surveyors since inception, serving Nigeria, Commonwealth countries and Sub-Saharan Africa. Currently under Federal Ministry of Works, there are proposals to upgrade to Federal University of Geomatics.

**Core Mandate:** To train professional land surveyors, geoinformatics specialists and survey technologists providing surveying manpower for national development and Africa.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide National Diploma in Surveying and Geoinformatics
(b) Award Higher National Diploma in Surveying and Geoinformatics
(c) Train land surveyors in cadastral surveying and land administration
(d) Provide training in engineering surveying for construction and infrastructure
(e) Offer training in geodetic surveying and geodesy
(f) Conduct training in hydrographic surveying for marine and coastal surveys
(g) Provide training in photogrammetry and remote sensing
(h) Offer training in Geographic Information Systems (GIS) and spatial data management
(i) Conduct training in cartography and map production
(j) Provide training in quantity surveying and valuation
(k) Offer training in mining surveying and underground measurements
(l) Conduct training in geoinformatics and spatial analysis
(m) Provide training in Global Navigation Satellite Systems (GNSS/GPS)
(n) Offer training in digital mapping and computer-aided design
(o) Conduct field training and practical surveying exercises
(p) Maintain surveying equipment including total stations, GPS receivers and levels
(q) Prepare graduates for registration with Surveyors Council of Nigeria (SURCON)
(r) Conduct applied research in surveying and geoinformatics
(s) Provide consultancy services on surveying and mapping projects
(t) Train surveyors for monitoring environmental disasters, area mapping for security, and development planning

---

## 176. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF MARINE AND BLUE ECONOMY - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 254001001

The Federal Ministry of Marine and Blue Economy is newly created ministry established in August 2023 by President Bola Tinubu to harness Nigeria's maritime resources, develop blue economy and maximize economic potential of Nigeria's 853km coastline, territorial waters, Exclusive Economic Zone and inland waterways. Previously maritime affairs were handled by Federal Ministry of Transportation. The Ministry coordinates development of ocean economy including fisheries, aquaculture, shipping, offshore oil and gas, marine tourism, seabed mining and marine biotechnology. Led by Minister of Marine and Blue Economy appointed 2023 assisted by Minister of State and Permanent Secretary. The Ministry coordinates Nigerian Maritime Administration and Safety Agency (NIMASA), Nigerian Ports Authority (NPA), National Inland Waterways Authority (NIWA), and implements Cabotage Act promoting indigenous shipping.

**Core Mandate:** To develop and regulate marine resources, promote sustainable blue economy development, ensure maritime security and maximize economic benefits from Nigeria's maritime domain.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national marine and blue economy policies
(b) Develop blue economy master plan harnessing maritime resources
(c) Coordinate maritime transport and shipping industry development
(d) Oversee Nigerian Maritime Administration and Safety Agency (NIMASA) for shipping regulation
(e) Promote indigenous shipping and cabotage regime implementation
(f) Regulate ports and port operations through Nigerian Ports Authority (NPA)
(g) Develop port infrastructure and maritime logistics
(h) Coordinate inland waterways development through National Inland Waterways Authority
(i) Promote maritime security and combat piracy, sea robbery and illegal fishing
(j) Coordinate marine fisheries development and aquaculture
(k) Regulate offshore oil and gas operations in territorial waters
(l) Promote seabed mining and marine mineral resources exploitation
(m) Develop marine tourism and coastal tourism
(n) Regulate maritime labour and seafarers welfare
(o) Promote ship building and ship repair industry
(p) Coordinate maritime education and training through maritime academies
(q) Manage maritime safety and search and rescue operations
(r) Promote marine environmental protection and ocean conservation
(s) Coordinate hydrographic surveys and nautical charting
(t) Maximize revenue generation from maritime sector

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 121-140

## 121. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF FRESH WATER FISHERIES TECHNOLOGY - NEW BUSSA

**MDA Code:** 254009001

The Federal College of Freshwater Fisheries Technology, New Bussa, Niger State, was established in January 1978 as Federal Freshwater Fisheries School under the aegis of Kainji Lake Research Institute (KLRI), initially located at temporary site in Shagunu. Following reorganization of research institutes nationwide in 1987, when Kainji Lake Research Institute (a multi-disciplinary research institute) became a single commodity (Fisheries) Institute under the National Institute for Freshwater Fisheries Research (NIFFR), the college's name changed from Federal Freshwater Fisheries School to Federal College of Freshwater Fisheries Technology in line with similar agricultural sector colleges. The college became self-accounting from January 1989, enabling direct project funding and addressing NBTE deficiencies before accreditation visits. Located in New Bussa, Niger State, the college operates under National Institute for Freshwater Fisheries Research (NIFFR) supervision and is accredited by National Board for Technical Education (NBTE), having been granted approval to mount Pre-ND (2008), ND (2010), and HND (2013) programmes in Fisheries Technology. The college's core mandate is to train junior, intermediate and senior fisheries personnel for inland fisheries development through short and long term formal education, and to train and retrain middle-level manpower in aquaculture technology. Specific functions include: (a) to train junior, intermediate and senior fisheries personnel for inland fisheries development through short and long term formal education; (b) to train and retrain middle-level manpower in aquaculture technology; (c) to encourage women participation in fisheries through post-harvest technology training; (d) to train food technicians, technologists and chemical analysts in preservation, processing, packaging, storage and marketing of food commodities and equipment; (e) to train agricultural extension technicians to carry out extension work among rural farmers and fisher folks; (f) to organize agricultural cooperatives and exhibitions, marketing of farm produce, tools, equipment and assisting in agricultural management research; (g) to train agricultural technicians capable of applying modern farming techniques in direct agricultural production; (h) to train caterers capable of producing acceptable local and continental dishes; and (i) to be role model for fisheries, agricultural and allied tertiary institutions developing highly intellectual, skilled, creative and self-employable individuals through modern scientific, technological, research and development methods for socio-economic and political growth toward national development goals.

---

## 120. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF FRESH WATER FISHERIES - BAGA

**MDA Code:** 254010001

The Federal College of Freshwater Fisheries Technology Baga, Borno State, was established by Federal Government of Nigeria under Lake Chad Research Institute in 1977 as a monotechnic institution to provide middle-level manpower in fisheries for Lake Chad basin. Originally established by proxy of Research Institute Order of 1975 (Supplement to Official Gazette No. 61, Vol. 62 of 11th December 1975 Part B) which established Lake Chad Research Institute as parent institute, the college is now under supervision of National Institute for Freshwater Fisheries Research (NIFFR), New Bussa, Niger State, following major reorganization of research institutes nationwide between 1988-1989. Located along Lake Chad shores in Baga, characterized by highly productive lake with historical irrational exploitation and inadequate knowledge of harvesting, preservation, processing and storage, the college admitted first students in 1979 for Vocational Competency in Fisheries, with immediate view of upgrading to OND and eventually HND. National Board for Technical Education halted further OND admission in 1989 pending appreciable upgrade of facilities and personnel, regularized with approval to mount Pre-ND (2008), ND (2010), and HND (2013) programmes in Fisheries Technology. The college's core mandate is to produce middle and lower level manpower in fisheries including fisheries artisans, aquaculture assistants, fisheries technicians and fisheries technologists capable of applying modern fisheries technology to boost fish production on sustainable level. Specific functions include: (a) to train junior, intermediate and senior fisheries personnel for inland fisheries development through short and long term formal education; (b) to train and retrain middle-level manpower in aquaculture technology; (c) to encourage women participation in fisheries through post-harvest technology training; (d) to train food technicians, technologists and chemical analysts in preservation, processing, packaging, storage and marketing of food commodities and equipment; (e) to train agricultural extension technicians to carry out extension work among rural farmers and fisher folks; (f) to provide training in fish farming, hatchery management and pond construction; (g) to educate students in fish biology, aquatic ecology and limnology; (h) to train personnel in fishing gear technology, boat building and fish capture methods; and (i) to be role model for fisheries, agricultural and allied tertiary institutions developing highly intellectual, skilled, creative and self-employable individuals through modern scientific, technological, research and development methods for socio-economic and political growth toward national development goals.

---

## 114. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF FISHERIES AND MARINE TECHNOLOGY - LAGOS

**MDA Code:** 254011001

The Federal College of Fisheries and Marine Technology, Lagos, assumed its present status as a full-fledged technological institution in 1992, having been established in 1969 as Federal School of Fisheries. Located at 2 Wilmot Point Road, Bar Beach, Victoria Island, Lagos, the college operates as a monotechnic approved by the National Board for Technical Education (NBTE). The transformation from vocational training institute to composite College of Technology resulted from comprehensive re-examination of training scope necessitated by phenomenal growth of Nigeria's fisheries and maritime sector requiring higher level indigenous expertise. The college's core mandate is to train middle-level manpower in areas of Fisheries Technology (freshwater, brackish and marine), including aquaculture, fish processing, fishing gear design and construction, and to train deep-sea fishermen to handle dermasal and pelagic ships, alongside interwoven training of maritime professionals (traditional seafarers and shore managers) supporting national and international maritime trade based on International Maritime Organisation (IMO) Safer Shipping and Cleaner Ocean principles. Specific functions include: (a) to provide tertiary-level training in Fisheries Technology, Marine Engineering and Nautical Science through National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND) programmes; (b) to train fisheries technicians and technologists in freshwater, brackish water and marine fisheries; (c) to provide aquaculture technology training including fish farming and hatchery management; (d) to train personnel in fish processing, preservation, packaging and storage; (e) to provide fishing gear design, construction and maintenance training; (f) to train deep-sea fishermen for dermasal and pelagic fishing operations; (g) to provide Maritime Transport and Business Management education; (h) to train marine engineers and nautical science professionals; (i) to train industrial and labour relations personnel for maritime sector; (j) to conduct research in fisheries and marine technology; and (k) to provide consultancy services in fisheries and maritime fields. Supervised by Federal Ministry of Agriculture through Agricultural Research Council of Nigeria (ARCN), the college has benefited from institutional support from FAO and JICA, with graduates dominating shipping and industrial fishing sub-sectors.

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL MINISTRY MANDATES: AGENCIES 161-180

## 161. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF ARTS, CULTURE AND CREATIVE ECONOMY - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 256001001

The Federal Ministry of Art, Culture, Tourism and Creative Economy (FMACTCE) was established on August 21, 2023, by President Bola Ahmed Tinubu, carved out from the Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. On October 23, 2024, it was consolidated through merger with the Federal Ministry of Tourism. The Ministry is led by Hannatu Musa Musawa as Minister. The Ministry launched "Destination 2030, Nigeria Everywhere" on November 21, 2023, targeting 10% GDP contribution from creative sector by 2030 and creation of 2 million jobs by 2027. The Ministry oversees 13 agencies including National Theatre (renamed Wole Soyinka Centre for Culture and Creative Arts in July 2024), National Gallery of Arts, National Commission for Museums and Monuments, National Institute for Cultural Orientation, National Council for Arts and Culture, and Nigerian Copyright Commission.

**Core Mandate:** To promote and develop arts, culture, creative industries and tourism, using creativity to boost economy, preserve cultural heritage and share Nigeria's culture globally.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Create enabling environment encouraging artistic excellence, promoting diversity and creativity
(b) Preserve Nigeria's rich cultural heritage across 371 tribes and over 250 ethnic groups
(c) Facilitate economic diversification through creative economy, wealth creation and value addition
(d) Implement Destination 2030 initiative targeting $100 billion GDP increase by 2030
(e) Develop and implement National Tourism Policy and National Tourism Masterplan
(f) Promote public-private partnerships for tourism infrastructure development
(g) Enhance tourist sites, train professional tour guides and promote community-based tourism
(h) Support artists, cultural groups and creative businesses
(i) Position Nigeria as premier destination for cultural tourism
(j) Renovate and maintain cultural institutions including National Theatre and cultural centers
(k) Promote Nigerian music, film (Nollywood), fashion, literature and visual arts globally
(l) Protect intellectual property rights of Nigerian creatives through Nigerian Copyright Commission
(m) Organize cultural festivals, exhibitions and events showcasing Nigeria's heritage
(n) Develop creative hubs and incubation centers supporting creative entrepreneurs
(o) Collaborate with state governments, private sector and international partners

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 181-200

## 181. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF SOLID MINERALS DEVELOPMENT - HQTRS

**MDA Code:** 257001001

The Federal Ministry of Solid Minerals Development (formerly Ministry of Mines and Steel Development, renamed to emphasize solid minerals sector) was established in 1985 to unlock economic potentials of Nigeria's solid minerals sub-sector. The Ministry operates under Nigerian Minerals and Mining Act 2007 which repealed Minerals and Mining Act 1999, vesting all property in and control of mineral resources in Federal Government. Led by Minister of Solid Minerals Development (current: Dr. Oladele Henry Alake), the Ministry implements National Minerals and Metals Policy and Roadmap for Growth and Development of Nigerian Mining Industry (2016). The Ministry manages Mining Cadastre Office (MCO) as independent agency for mineral titles administration.

**Core Mandate:** To exploit Nigeria's mineral endowments in environmentally sustainable manner and establish vibrant minerals and metals industry for wealth creation, poverty reduction, economic growth and GDP contribution.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national solid minerals development policies and strategies
(b) Administer Nigerian Minerals and Mining Act 2007 and Minerals and Mining Regulations 2011
(c) Issue mineral titles including reconnaissance permits, exploration licences, small-scale mining leases, mining leases and quarry leases through Mining Cadastre Office
(d) Collect rents, fees and royalties from solid minerals exploitation
(e) Maintain Mining Cadastre cadastral maps and records of mineral title locations and ownership
(f) Conduct geological surveys through Nigerian Geological Survey Agency identifying mineral deposits
(g) Operate Mines Inspectorate Department inspecting mining operations for safety and standards compliance
(h) Enforce environmental compliance through Mines Environmental Compliance Department
(i) Regulate artisanal and small-scale mining operations
(j) Promote investment in mining sector and attract foreign direct investment
(k) Manage Solid Minerals Development Fund for sector financing
(l) Develop mining infrastructure and mineral processing facilities
(m) Regulate exploitation of gold, coal, bitumen, limestone, iron ore, lead-zinc, barites and other minerals
(n) Ensure sustainable mining practices and environmental protection
(o) Coordinate Federal Mines Offices in all 36 states and FCT
(p) Promote local content and value addition in mining sector
(q) Combat illegal mining and mineral smuggling
(r) Facilitate technology transfer and mining skills development
(s) Maintain mineral title database and geo-mining information systems
(t) Ensure mining sector contribution to GDP and economic diversification

## 175. FEDERAL MINISTRY OF LIVESTOCK DEVELOPMENT

**MDA Code:** 265001001

The Federal Ministry of Livestock Development is newly created ministry established in October 2024 by President Bola Tinubu during cabinet reshuffle to address persistent farmer-herder conflicts, modernize livestock sector and promote livestock industry development. Previously livestock development was handled by Department of Livestock under Federal Ministry of Agriculture. The creation of standalone Livestock Ministry represents significant policy shift recognizing livestock sector's economic potential and need for dedicated attention to pastoralism challenges including nomadic herding, grazing routes, cattle rustling, and conflicts between farmers and herders that have caused thousands of deaths. Led by Minister of Livestock Development appointed October 2024. The Ministry is expected to coordinate livestock production, animal health services, ranching development, dairy industry, meat production, pastoral development and implementation of National Livestock Transformation Plan.

**Core Mandate:** To develop and regulate livestock sector, promote modern livestock production systems, resolve farmer-herder conflicts and enhance livestock contribution to national economy and food security.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Formulate and implement national livestock development policies
(b) Promote modern livestock production systems replacing open grazing
(c) Develop and implement National Livestock Transformation Plan
(d) Establish livestock grazing reserves and ranches nationwide
(e) Coordinate ranching development and livestock settlements
(f) Resolve farmer-herder conflicts through sustainable grazing solutions
(g) Regulate nomadic pastoralism and promote sedentary livestock rearing
(h) Develop cattle colonies and grazing routes
(i) Promote dairy industry development and milk production
(j) Coordinate meat production, slaughterhouses and meat processing
(k) Oversee animal health services and veterinary services
(l) Coordinate disease control in livestock including vaccination programmes
(m) Regulate livestock trade and cattle markets
(n) Combat cattle rustling and livestock theft
(o) Promote livestock insurance schemes
(p) Develop livestock breeding programmes and genetic improvement
(q) Coordinate feeds and fodder production
(r) Promote poultry production and egg production
(s) Develop small ruminants (goats and sheep) production
(t) Coordinate livestock research through research institutes

## 83. Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology, Ibadan
**MDA Code:** 265004001

The Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology, Ibadan is located at Moore Plantation, Apata, Ibadan, Oyo State. It operates as a parastatal under the Federal Ministry of Livestock Development. The institution was established to address the shortage of trained personnel for livestock disease diagnosis and control in the former Western Region of Nigeria, particularly for poultry diseases.

**Core Mandate and Functions:**
The college mandate is to produce well-trained and morally sound graduates to become self-employed in the areas of Science and Agriculture. It aims to produce graduates at National Diploma and Higher National Diploma levels to meet the needs of farmers, agro-allied industries, pharmaceutical companies, and government agencies. Core functions include: producing technicians capable of carrying out routine preventive measures and advisory services in livestock, fishery, and crop production; establishing linkages and collaboration with policy makers, research institutes, and training institutions; enabling graduates to establish and manage modern standard livestock farms, veterinary clinics, and diagnostic laboratories; promoting capacity building through outreach programmes, vocational training, and continuous education; and promoting youth development and empowerment opportunities through entrepreneurial and home economics activities. The college is the only Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology in the southern part of Nigeria.

---

## 84. Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology, Vom
**MDA Code:** 265005001

The Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology, Vom is located within the National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI), Vom, Jos South Local Government Area, Plateau State. The college was established in 1941 as the Veterinary School, Vom, with an initial intake of ten students. It has the distinction of being the first higher institution in West Africa to produce graduate Veterinary Surgeons before the first university was established in Nigeria at Ibadan in 1948.

In March 1980, the Veterinary School was formally accorded college status and renamed College of Animal Health and Husbandry with a mandate to award Ordinary and Higher Diplomas. The National Science and Technology Amendment Decree No. 46 of December 30, 1990, gave legal backing to conduct training for technicians and technologists. The college was renamed Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology on January 29, 1991.

**Core Mandate and Functions:**
The college is mandated to train middle-level manpower in Animal Health, Animal Production, Livestock Extension/Management Services, and Vocational Training who can effectively utilise their training to establish and manage livestock farms. Its guiding principles include: improving service to the livestock sector through effective training; training minds to appreciate the significance of animal resources in national development; inculcating the importance of practical self-commitment to the dignity of labour for national self-sufficiency in animal protein production; and producing extension staff who can bring agro-veterinary research and innovation to livestock farmers and feedback farmers' problems to appropriate authorities. Programmes include Pre-ND, ND and HND in Animal Health and Production Technology, HND in Animal Health, HND in Animal Production, HND in Agricultural Extension and Management, and Veterinary Science Laboratory Technology.

---

## 126. FEDERAL COLLEGE OF VETERINARY AND MEDICAL LABORATORY TECHNOLOGY, VOM, PLATEAU STATE

**MDA Code:** 265006001

The Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology (also known as Federal College of Veterinary and Medical Laboratory Technology), Vom, is one of Federal Colleges of Agriculture located within National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI), Vom, Jos South LGA, Plateau State, at altitude of 1,285 meters above sea level. The college was established in 1941 as Veterinary School, Vom, with ten students, becoming first Higher Institution in West Africa to produce graduate Veterinary Surgeons before University of Ibadan was established in 1948. Initially offering Veterinary Assistants (junior course, senior course) and Veterinary Officers training, the school discontinued Veterinary Officers' course in June 1950 when University College Ibadan assumed higher veterinary training, concentrating thereafter on 2-year Veterinary Assistants' Course. In March 1980, Veterinary School was formally accorded college status and renamed College of Animal Health and Husbandry with mandate to award Ordinary and Higher Diplomas. National Science and Technology Amendment Decree No. 46 of December 30, 1990, gave legal backing to Research Institute to conduct training for technicians and technologists for award of National Diploma (ND), Higher National Diploma (HND) and Certificates. NBTE granted accreditation for ND programmes in Animal Health and Production in 1989 and HND programmes in 1992, with college renamed Federal College of Animal Health and Production Technology in 1991. In 2014, department of Veterinary Science Laboratory Technology (VSLT) was established with options in Veterinary Microbiology, Pathology, Radiology and Anaesthesiology, graduating and inducting 162 maiden graduands of Veterinary Laboratory Technology and Animal Health Technology into Veterinary Council of Nigeria (VCN) as professional para-veterinarians in 2021. The college's core mandate is to train middle-level manpower in Animal Health, Animal Production, Livestock Extension/Management Services, Veterinary Laboratory Technology and Vocational Training who can effectively utilize training to establish and manage livestock farms. Specific functions include: (a) to provide Preliminary National Diploma in Science and Technology (1 year); (b) to offer National Diploma (ND) in Animal Health and Production Technology (2 years); (c) to provide Higher National Diploma (HND) in Animal Health (2 years) with veterinary laboratory technology specialization; (d) to offer Higher National Diploma (HND) in Animal Production (2 years); (e) to provide Higher National Diploma (HND) in Veterinary Science Laboratory Technology with options in Veterinary Microbiology, Pathology, Radiology and Anaesthesiology; (f) to train extension staff capable of bringing agro-veterinary research and innovations to livestock farmers and feeding back farmers' problems to authorities; (g) to produce men and women knowledgeable in animal production who can establish and manage livestock farms to maximum advantage; (h) to train para-veterinarians and veterinary laboratory technologists; (i) to offer degree programmes in Agricultural Science (Animal Production) through affiliation with Abubakar Tafawa Balewa University, Bauchi; (j) to provide Post Graduate Diploma in Animal Health and Production; (k) to offer short courses in animal health, poultry production, beef and dairy production, small ruminants production, and agro-forestry; (l) to produce job-providers not job-seekers; and (m) to award certificates preparing graduates for induction into Veterinary Council of Nigeria and National Youth Service Corps mobilization.
